\displaystyle\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{div }(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5}^{2}+\|\text{curl }(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5}^{2}+|\overline{\partial}{}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{3}^{2},

(3.60)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4.5-k}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3.5-k}^{2}+\|\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3.5-k}^{2}+|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}v^{3}|_{3-k}^{2},

(3.61)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5-k}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5-k}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5-k}^{2}+|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{3-k}^{2}.

(3.62)

Here, notice that we do not pick up the terms on
\Gamma_{0}
since
v^{3}=0
and
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}=b_{0}^{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\eta^{3}=0
there.
Also, the
L^{2}
-norms in (
3.59
) and (
3.60
) are controlled by energy conservation law. We will omit the control of
L^{2}
-norms appearing in the div-curl estimates in the rest of this manuscript.

Divergence estimates

For the velocity vector field, one has

\text{div }v=\underbrace{\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v}_{=0}+(\delta^{\mu\alpha}-\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}=\text{div }_{Id-\tilde{a}}v,

(3.63)

and thus

\|\text{div }v\|_{3.5}\leq\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v\|_{3.5}+\|(\delta^{\mu\alpha}-\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\|_{3.5}\leq 0+\varepsilon\|v\|_{4.5}.

(3.64)

Time differentiating (
3.63
), one has

\displaystyle\|\text{div }\partial_{t}v\|_{2.5}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\text{div }_{Id-\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}v\|_{2.5}+\|\text{div }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{a}}v\|_{2.5}

(3.65)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2.5}+\|\partial_{t}\tilde{a}\|_{2.5}\|v\|_{3.5}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2.5}+\|\partial\tilde{\eta}\|_{2.5}\|v\|_{3.5}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2.5}+P(\|v_{0}\|_{3.5})+\|\eta\|_{3.5}\int_{0}^{T}P(\|v\|_{4.5}),

where in the last step we write
\|v\|_{3.5}
in terms of initial data plus time integral and use Young’s inequality. The divergence estimates of
\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{3.5-k}
,
k=2,3
are parallel and so we omit the details.

\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1.5}+\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0.5}\lesssim\varepsilon(\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2.5}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5})+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(3.66)

As for
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
,
one no longer has
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)=0
due to the tangential mollification.
Instead, one can compute the evolution equation verified by
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)
. Invoking
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v=0
and
\partial_{t}\eta=v
, we have

\partial_{t}(\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta))=[\text{div}_{\tilde{a}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]v+\text{div }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta.

(3.67)

The commutator
[\text{div}_{\tilde{a}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]v
only contains first order derivative of
v
and
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
. Using the identity

\displaystyle\partial\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}=-\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\partial\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha},

(3.68)

which follows from differentiating
\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\tilde{\eta}_{\beta}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}
, one has

\displaystyle[\text{div}_{\tilde{a}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]v=

\displaystyle\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}b_{0}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}-b_{0}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}b_{0}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha}+\partial_{\beta}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma})\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}-\partial_{\beta}b_{0}^{\nu}\underbrace{\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}}_{\delta_{\nu}^{\mu}}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{\beta}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}_{\gamma})\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}.

(3.69)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\text{div }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta=\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}=-a^{\mu\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\tilde{v}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{\mu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}.

(3.70)

Taking
\partial^{3.5}
in (
3.67
) and testing it with
\partial^{3.5}\text{div}\,_{\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
, we get

\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5}^{2}\leq\|\text{div }b_{0}\|_{3.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5}\left(\|[\text{div}_{\tilde{a}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]v\|_{3.5}+\|\text{div }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5}\right).

(3.71)

This suggests that we need to control

\int_{0}^{T}\|[\text{div}_{\tilde{a}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]v\|_{3.5}

and

\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{div }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5}
on the right hand side.
In light of (
3.69
) and (
3.70
), we have

\int_{0}^{T}\|[\text{div}_{\tilde{a}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]v\|_{3.5}+\|\text{div }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5}^{2}\leq\|\text{div}\,b_{0}\|_{3.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(3.72)

which implies, after invoking (
3.19
), that

\|\text{div }(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(3.73)

Similarly, one can take
\partial^{3.5-k}\partial_{t}^{k}
for
1\leq k\leq 3
in (
3.67
), then compute the
L^{2}
estimates to get

\|\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5-k}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5-k}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(3.74)

Curl estimates

The curl estimates can be derived by the evolution equation of
\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}v
. Taking
\text{curl}_{\tilde{a}}
in the second equation of (
3.2
), we get

\partial_{t}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}v)-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)=\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{A}}v+[\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)](b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta.

(3.75)

Then we take
\partial^{3.5}
, test it with
\partial^{3.5}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}v)
and integrate
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
by parts (recall that
b_{0}\cdot N|_{\partial\Omega}=0
and
\text{div }b_{0}=0
) to get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\partial^{3.5}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}v|^{2}+|\partial^{3.5}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta|^{2}\,dy

(3.76)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\left(\left[\partial^{3.5},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\right]\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta+\partial^{3.5}\left(\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{A}}v+[\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)](b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right)(\partial^{3.5}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}v)\,dy

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}\partial^{3.5}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\cdot\left(\left[\partial^{3.5}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\right]v+\partial^{3.5}(\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{A}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\right)\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(\|b_{0}\|_{4.5},\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5},\|v\|_{4.5},\|\eta\|_{4.5})\lesssim\mathcal{P},

and thus by the a priori assumption (
3.8
), we have

\|\text{curl }v\|_{3.5}^{2}+\|\text{curl }(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}(\|v\|_{4.5}^{2}+\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5}^{2})+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}\,dt.

(3.77)

Similarly, replacing
\partial^{3.5}
by
\partial^{3.5-k}\partial_{t}^{k}
for
1\leq k\leq 3
, we can similarly get the following curl estimates

\|\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5-k}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5-k}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}\,dt.

(3.78)

3.3
Boundary estimates

We need to control the boundary term
|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}v\cdot N|_{3-k}
and
|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\cdot N|_{3-k}.
In the case of zero surface tension, one can use the normal trace theorem to reduce
|\overline{\partial}{}X\cdot N|_{s-1.5}
to the interior tangential estimates
\|\overline{\partial}{}^{s}X\|_{0}
. But the interior tangential estimates, especially in the full spatial derivative case, cannot be controlled due to the appearance of surface tension.

3.3.1
Control of
|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}v\cdot N|_{3-k}

Theorem 3.7
.

For
k=0,1,2,3
, one has

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}v^{3}|_{3-k}^{2}\lesssim|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\overline{\partial}{}^{3-k}\partial_{t}^{k}v)|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(3.79)

First we study the case when
k=3
. Let us consider the projection of
\partial_{t}^{3}v
to the Eulerian normal direction, i.e.,
(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)^{3}
instead of Lagrangian normal direction. The reason is twofold.

1.

Recall that (
2.6
) in Lemma
2.1
gives that

\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Delta_{g}\eta^{\alpha}=\sigma\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{ij}^{2}\eta^{\lambda}.

So if we test
\partial_{t}^{4}
-differentiated version of (
2.6
) with
\partial_{t}^{4}v
and integrate by parts, then the term
|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)|_{0}^{2}
is produced as part of energy term,i.e.,

\int_{\Gamma}\sigma\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{ij}^{2}\eta^{\lambda}\cdot\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}=-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Gamma}\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)\right|^{2}\,dS+\cdots

(3.80)

2.

The difference between
X^{3}
and
(\Pi X)^{3}
is expected to be small within a short period of time.

We will make the above assertions precise. For any vector field
X
, the following identity holds:

\displaystyle X^{3}=\delta^{3}_{\lambda}X^{\lambda}=

\displaystyle(\delta^{3}_{\lambda}-g^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda})X^{\lambda}+g^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda}X^{\lambda}

(3.81)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi^{3}_{\lambda}X^{\lambda}+g^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda}X^{\lambda}=(\Pi X)^{3}+g^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda}X^{\lambda}.

Using
\overline{\partial}{}\eta^{3}=\int_{0}^{T}\overline{\partial}{}v^{3}\,dt
(this is true since
\overline{\partial}{}\eta^{3}=0
initially), we can control the difference between
(\Pi X)^{3}
and
X^{3}
as

\displaystyle\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left((\Pi X)^{3}-X^{3}\right)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|g^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}X^{\lambda}\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}(g^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda})X^{\lambda}\right|_{0}^{2}

(3.82)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(|\overline{\partial}{}\eta|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\partial}{}X|_{0}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\overline{\partial}{}v^{3}\right|_{1.5}^{2}\,dt+|X|_{L^{4}}^{2}|\overline{\partial}{}(g^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda})|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|X\|_{1.5}^{2}P(|\overline{\partial}{}\eta|_{L^{\infty}})\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Let
X=\partial_{t}^{3}v
. Since
\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}
is included in the energy
E_{\kappa}^{(1)}
, then (
3.82
) implies

\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left((\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)^{3}-\partial_{t}^{3}v^{3}\right)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(3.83)

and thus

\displaystyle\left|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{3}\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)\right|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(3.84)

Finally, (
3.79
) follows from a parallel argument by assigning
X=\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}v,\overline{\partial}{}^{3}v
, respectively.

3.3.2
Control of
|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\cdot N|_{3-k}

First, when
k\geq 1
, the control of
|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\cdot N|_{3-k}
requires to that of
|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{l}v\cdot N|_{3-l}
(modulo lower order terms generated when derivatives land on
b_{0}
) for
l=0,1,2
, which has been done in the previous subsection.

Thus it suffices to study the control of
|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{4}
. In Luo-Zhang
[
38
]
, the boundary condition forms an elliptic equation
-\sigma\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta^{\alpha}=a^{3\alpha}q
and thus one can take
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
and then use elliptic estimates. However, the boundary condition now takes the form (
3.6
) in the smoothed approximate equations and it appears that there is no appropriate boundary
H^{2}
-control for
\kappa(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\overline{\Delta}{}(v\cdot\tilde{n})
. Specifically, it does not seem to be possible to control
|\kappa(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\overline{\Delta}{}(v\cdot\tilde{n})|_{2}
by
\mathcal{P}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)E_{\kappa}(T)+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
due to the lack of time integrals.

Our strategy here is to adapt the inequality (
3.82
) with
X=\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left((\Pi\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)^{3}-\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}\right)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1.5}^{2}P(|\overline{\partial}{}\eta|_{L^{\infty}})\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}\lesssim\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(3.85)

where the last inequality holds since
\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5}^{2}
is included in
E_{\kappa}^{(1)}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\right|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(3.86)

Remark
.

The term
\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\right|_{0}^{2}
is part of the energy
E_{\kappa}^{(1)}
defined in (
3.9
), which is a positive term generated by the
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
tangential energy estimate (See Section
4
). There is no problem to study the
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
-differentiated equations (
3.2
) since it is analogous to the
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}
-differentiated equations. Indeed, as mentioned in the remark right after (
1.17
) that
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
and
\partial_{t}\eta
(which is
v
) have the same space-time regularity.

4
Tangential energy estimates

The purpose of this section is to investigate the a priori energy estimate for the tangentially differentiated approximate
\kappa
-problem (
3.2
). In particular, we will study the energy estimate for

\partial_{t}^{4},\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3},\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2},\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t},\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)

differentiated
\kappa
-problem, respectively.

4.1
Control of full time derivatives

Now we compute the
L^{2}
-estimate of
\partial_{t}^{4}v
and
\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
. This turns out to be the most difficult case compared to the cases with at least one tangential spatial derivative that will be treated in Section
4.2
. This is because
\partial_{t}^{4}v
can only be controlled in
L^{2}(\Omega)
and so one has to control some higher-order interior terms instead. These interior terms will be treated by adapting the geometric cancellation scheme introduced in
[
13
]
together with an error term which can be controlled by terms in
E_{\kappa}^{(3)}(t)
.

For the sake of simplicity and clean arguments, we shall focus on treating the leading order terms. We henceforth adopt:

Notation 4.1
.

We use
\overset{L}{=}
to denote equality modulo error terms that are effective of lower order. For instance,
X\overset{L}{=}Y
means that
X=Y+\mathcal{R}
, where
\mathcal{R}
consists of lower order terms with respect to
Y
.

Invoking (
3.2
) and integrating
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
by parts, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}^{4}v|^{2}+\left|\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right|^{2}\,dy

(4.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{5}v^{\alpha}\,dy\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v^{\alpha}\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta_{\alpha}\,dy\,dt-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}q)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v^{\alpha}\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}\,dy\,dt-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}q)\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v^{\alpha}\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}q)\,dy\,dt=:I.

Then we integrate
\partial_{\mu}
by parts,
I
becomes

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}q)-\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}q)}_{I_{0}}+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{0}}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}q)}_{I_{0}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}[\partial_{t}^{4},\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}]q}_{I_{1}}+I_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\underbrace{\partial_{t}^{4}\text{div}\,_{\tilde{A}}v}_{=0}\partial_{t}^{4}q-\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}[\partial_{t}^{4},\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}]\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q}_{L}+I_{1}+I_{0}+I_{0}^{\prime}.

(4.2)

I_{0}^{\prime}=0
since on
\Gamma_{0}
, we have
\tilde{A}^{31}=\tilde{A}^{32}=0
,
\tilde{A}^{33}=1
, and
v_{3}=0
.

I_{I}
yields a top order interior term when all
4
time derivatives land on
\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}
, i.e.,

I_{11}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha})q.

(4.3)

If
\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}
were
A^{\mu\alpha}
then this term could be controlled by adapting the cancellation scheme developed in
[
13
]
. This motivate us to consider

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}(\partial_{t}^{4}A^{\mu\alpha})q+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\Big(\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}-A^{\mu\alpha})\Big)q=I_{111}+I_{112}.

(4.4)

Invoking (
3.24
), we have
\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{A}=\sum_{i+j=3}b_{ij}(\partial_{t}^{i}\partial\tilde{\eta})(\partial\partial_{t}^{j}\tilde{v})
and
\partial_{t}^{4}A=\sum_{i+j=3}b_{ij}^{\prime}(\partial_{t}^{i}\partial\eta)\partial\partial_{t}^{j}v
, where we denoted
A^{\mu\alpha}
by
A
and
\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}
by
\tilde{A}
by a slight abuse of notations. These imply that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}-A)=\sum_{i+j=3}b_{ij}\partial_{t}^{i}\partial\tilde{\eta}\partial\partial_{t}^{j}(\tilde{v}-v)+\sum_{i+j=3}b_{ij}^{\prime}\partial_{t}^{i}(\partial\tilde{\eta}-\partial\eta)\partial\partial_{t}^{j}v,

and so
\|\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}-A)\|_{0}

consists the sum of
\|i_{\ell}\|_{0}
,
\ell=1,\cdots,8
, where

\displaystyle i_{1}=(\partial\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{v})\partial(\tilde{v}-v),\quad i_{2}=(\partial\partial_{t}\tilde{v})\partial\partial_{t}(\tilde{v}-v),\quad i_{3}=(\partial\tilde{v})\partial\partial_{t}^{2}(\tilde{v}-v),\quad i_{4}=(\partial\tilde{\eta})\partial\partial_{t}^{3}(\tilde{v}-v),

\displaystyle i_{5}=\partial\partial_{t}^{2}(\tilde{v}-v)\partial v,\quad i_{6}=\partial\partial_{t}(\tilde{v}-v)\partial\partial_{t}v,\quad i_{7}=\partial(\tilde{v}-v)\partial\partial_{t}^{2}v,\quad i_{8}=\partial(\tilde{\eta}-\eta)\partial\partial_{t}^{3}v.

The
L^{2}
-norm of these quantities can be controlled by invoking Lemma
3.4
. Specifically,

\displaystyle\|i_{1}\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial(\tilde{v}-v)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{v}\|_{0}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}\|v\|_{3.5}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1},

\displaystyle\|i_{2}\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial\partial_{t}(\tilde{v}-v)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial\partial_{t}\tilde{v}\|_{0}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3.5}\|\partial_{t}v\|_{1},

\displaystyle\|i_{3}\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial\partial_{t}^{2}(\tilde{v}-v)\|_{0}\|\partial\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1.5}\|v\|_{3},

\displaystyle\|i_{4}\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial\partial_{t}^{3}(\tilde{v}-v)\|_{0}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}\|\eta\|_{3}\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5},

and

\displaystyle\|i_{5}\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial\partial_{t}^{2}(\tilde{v}-v)\|_{0}\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1.5}\|v\|_{3},

\displaystyle\|i_{6}\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial\partial_{t}(\tilde{v}-v)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial\partial_{t}v\|_{0}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}\|\partial_{t}v\|_{3.5}\|\partial_{t}v\|_{1},

\displaystyle\|i_{7}\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial(\tilde{v}-v)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}\|v\|_{3.5}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{1},

\displaystyle\|i_{8}\|_{0}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial(\tilde{\eta}-\eta)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\sqrt{\kappa}\|\eta\|_{3.5}\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}.

Summing these up and moving
\sqrt{\kappa}
to
\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial v\|_{0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{112}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial v\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}-A^{\mu\alpha})\|_{0}\|q\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial v\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(4.5)

where the first term on the RHS contributes to
\varepsilon\mathcal{P}
, and we bound
\|q\|_{L^{\infty}}
by
\|q\|_{2}\leq\mathcal{P}
through (
3.36
).

We next control
I_{111}
.
The argument is largely similar to that used in Section 3.1.3 of
[
13
]
which relies on exploiting the geometric structure to create a remarkable cancellation scheme among the leading order terms.
Invoking (
3.24
) and then expanding the index
\mu
in
I_{111}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{111}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\partial_{3}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}+I_{low}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I_{1111}+I_{1112}+\cdots+I_{1116}+I_{low},

(4.6)

where
I_{low}
consists terms of the form
\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\partial\partial_{t}^{2}v\partial v\partial\partial_{t}^{3}v
. This term can be treated by integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\partial\partial_{t}^{2}v\partial v\partial\partial_{t}^{4}v=\int_{\Omega}q\partial\partial_{t}^{2}v\partial v\partial\partial_{t}^{3}v\Big|_{0}^{T}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(q\partial\partial_{t}^{2}v\partial v)\partial\partial_{t}^{3}v,

where the second term is controlled by
\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
, whereas

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}q\partial\partial_{t}^{2}v\partial v\partial\partial_{t}^{3}v\Big|_{0}^{T}\right|\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}^{2}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{0}^{2}\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}+\varepsilon||\partial_{t}^{3}v||_{1}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

To control the leading terms in (
4.6
), we consider
I_{1111}+I_{1112}
,
I_{1113}+I_{1114}
, and
I_{1115}+I_{1116}
. For
I_{1111}+I_{1112}
, integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts in
I_{1112}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1111}+I_{1112}\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}}_{=0}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\Big|_{0}^{T}+I_{low}^{\prime},

(4.7)

where
I_{low}^{\prime}
consists terms of the form
\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\partial_{t}(q\partial\eta)(\partial\partial_{t}^{3}v)^{2}
which can be controlled by
\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
. Next we treat the first term on the RHS of (
4.7
). It suffices to consider
-\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\Big|_{t=T}:=\mathcal{T}
as

\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{\tau}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\Big|_{t=0}\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}.

We shall drop
\Big|_{t=T}
in
\mathcal{T}
for the sake of clean notations. Expanding in
\tau
, we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}=-\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda i}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}-\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda 3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}.

(4.8)

Since
\partial_{3}\eta_{i}|_{t=0}=0
, we can write
\partial_{3}\eta_{i}=\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{3}v_{i}
, and so

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda i}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\leq\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.9)

In addition to this, we have
\partial_{3}\eta_{3}=1+\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{3}v_{3}
, and so

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda 3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\partial_{3}\eta_{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\leq-\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda 3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.10)

To treat the first term on the RHS, we expand
\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda 3}
and get

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}q\epsilon^{\alpha\lambda 3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}=-\int_{\Omega}q(\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{2}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{2}).

(4.11)

Integrating by parts
\overline{\partial}{}_{2}
in the first term and
\overline{\partial}{}_{1}
in the second term, we have

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}q(\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{2}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\Omega}q\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}-\int_{\Omega}q\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{2}}_{=0}+\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}q\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}-\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}q\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{2}.

Here,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}q\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}-\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}q\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{2}\Big|\lesssim\epsilon||\partial_{t}^{3}v||_{1}^{2}+||\partial_{t}^{3}v||_{0}^{2}||\partial q||_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\leq\varepsilon||\partial_{t}^{3}v||_{1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle I_{1111}+I_{1112}\leq\varepsilon E(T)+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.12)

On the other hand,
I_{1113}+I_{1114}
and
I_{1115}+I_{1116}
are treated similarly with only one exception. Previously, we integrated
\overline{\partial}{}_{1}
and
\overline{\partial}{}_{2}
by parts in (
4.11
) and so there were no boundary terms. However, when controlling
I_{1113}+I_{1114}
, we need to integrate
\overline{\partial}{}_{1}
and
\partial_{3}
by parts when treating (
4.11
), and thus the following boundary term will appear:

\int_{\Gamma}q\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{3}.

(4.13)

To control this term, we invoke the identity

\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{3}=\Pi_{\lambda}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}+g^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}=\Pi_{\lambda}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}+g^{kl}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}v^{3}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda},

(4.14)

and thus (
4.13
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}q\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}\Pi_{\lambda}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}+\int_{\Gamma}q\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}g^{kl}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}v^{3}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon|\Pi\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{0}^{2}+|q|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}|\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{0}^{2}+\left|q\partial_{t}^{3}v_{1}g^{kl}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}v^{3}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta_{\lambda}\right|_{0.5}|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}|_{-0.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon|\Pi\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{0}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

The extra term generated when analyzing
I_{1115}+I_{1116}
is of the same type integral and thus can be treated by the same method.
Therefore,

\displaystyle I_{111}\leq\varepsilon E(T)+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.15)

Next, we study

\displaystyle I_{1}-I_{11}=

\displaystyle 4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}q+6\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{2}q

(4.16)

\displaystyle+4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}q=I_{12}+I_{13}+I_{14}.

For
I_{12}
, we integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts and obtain

4\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}q-4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}q).

Here, the second term is
\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
, and since

\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}=Q(\partial\tilde{\eta})\partial\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{v}+\text{lower order terms}

then the first term is bounded by

\displaystyle\varepsilon||\partial_{t}^{3}v||_{1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

I_{13}
is treated by adapting a similar method and so we omit the details. However, we can’t integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts in order to control
I_{14}
as we do not have a bound for
\partial_{t}^{4}q
. We integrate
\partial_{\mu}
by parts instead.

\displaystyle I_{14}=4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}q-4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{\mu}(\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}q).

There is no problem to control the second integral by
\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
. For the first integral, invoking the boundary condition (
3.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle-4\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{g}}{\sqrt{\tilde{g}}}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}\Big)+4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\kappa\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{g}}}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\tilde{n})\Big)=I_{141}+I_{142}.

(4.17)

Invoking (
2.6
),
I_{141}
becomes

\displaystyle I_{141}=

\displaystyle-4\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{g}}{\sqrt{\tilde{g}}}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}\Big)

\displaystyle-4\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{g}}{\sqrt{\tilde{g}}}g^{ij}g^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta^{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}\Big).

It suffices for us to consider the first integral only since the second integral is of the same type. Integrating by parts
\overline{\partial}{}_{j}
first and then
\partial_{t}
, the first integral becomes

\displaystyle-4\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{g}}{\sqrt{\tilde{g}}}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n}\Big)-4\sigma\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{g}}{\sqrt{\tilde{g}}}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{2}v\cdot\tilde{n}\Big)+\mathcal{R}.

Since
\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{3.5}
is part of
E_{\kappa}^{(1)}(t)
, the trace lemma implies that the first integral is bounded straightforwardly by
\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
. Moreover, for the second integral, we have

\displaystyle 4\sigma\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{g}}{\sqrt{\tilde{g}}}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{2}v\cdot\tilde{n}\Big)

(4.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon|\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{1}^{2}+P(\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}})|\partial_{t}^{2}v|_{1}^{2}\leq\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

where we used the trace lemma in the last inequality.
In addition,

I_{142}\overset{L}{=}-4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha})\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{g}}}\overline{\Delta}{}(\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n})\Big).

Integrating
\overline{\partial}{}
by parts,then

\displaystyle I_{142}\overset{L}{=}

\displaystyle 4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha})\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{g}}}(\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle 4\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v|_{0}^{2}+P(\|\partial\eta\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}})|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{0}^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Now, we start to analyze the boundary integral
I_{0}
in (
4.2
). This is essentially identical to the case of the incompressible Euler equations, which have been treated in
[
11
]
, Sect.12. Indeed, as what appears in the previous paper
[
38
]
concerning the a priori estimate, we found that the magnetic field plays no role in the estimate of
I_{0}
. But we shall provide the control of the top order terms for the sake of the completeness of our proof.

By plugging the boundary condition

\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}q=-\sigma\sqrt{g}(\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n})\tilde{n}^{\alpha}+\kappa\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\tilde{n})\right)\tilde{n}^{\alpha}

in
I_{0}
we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma}I_{0}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha})\,dS\,dt-\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}[(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\tilde{n})\tilde{n}^{\alpha}]\,dS\,dt,

(4.19)

where, after integrating one tangential derivative by parts, the second term becomes

\displaystyle-\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\sum_{\ell=0,1}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{\ell}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}[\overline{\partial}{}^{\ell}(v\cdot\tilde{n})\tilde{n}^{\alpha}]\,dS\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}[\overline{\partial}{}^{\ell}(v\cdot\tilde{n})\overline{\partial}{}^{\ell}\tilde{n}^{\alpha}]\,dS\,dt\bigg).

(4.20)

The first term on the RHS contributes to the positive energy term (after moving to the LHS)

\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\left|\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\tilde{n}\right|_{1}^{2}\,dS\,dt

together with errors terms. The most difficult error term is

\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt,

(4.21)

where the other errors are either with the same type of integrand or are effectively of lower order by one derivative with the case above. Since
\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{n}=Q(\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta})\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\tilde{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle\overset{L}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}\cdot\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}P(|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}},|v|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}})|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v|_{0}|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n}|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v|_{0}^{2}+\sup_{t}P(|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}},|v|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}})+\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n}|_{0}^{2}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{2.5}^{2}\Big)^{2}+\sup_{t}P(|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}},|v|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E_{\kappa}^{(3)}+(E_{\kappa}^{(3)})^{2}+\sup_{t}P(|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}},|v|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}}).

Here, the last term can be controlled appropriately because

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma)}\lesssim\|\eta\|_{3}\leq\|\eta_{0}\|_{3}+\int_{0}^{T}\|v\|_{3},

\displaystyle|v|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}}\lesssim\|v\|_{2}\leq\|v_{0}\|_{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|v_{t}\|_{2},

and so
\sup_{t}P(|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}},|v|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}})\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
. In addition, the second term on the RHS of (
4.20
) can be treated by the same argument.

Next we analyze the first term on the RHS of (
4.19
).
Since
\hat{n}\cdot\hat{n}=1
, invoking (
2.5
) in Lemma
2.1
and we obtain

\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\hat{n}\hat{n}^{\alpha}=-\mathcal{H}\circ\eta\hat{n}^{\alpha}=\Delta_{g}\eta^{\alpha},

(4.22)

and so we are able to rewrite

\displaystyle\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha}=

\displaystyle\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\hat{n}\hat{n}^{\alpha}+\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}(\tilde{n}^{\alpha}-\hat{n}^{\alpha})+\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot(\tilde{n}-\hat{n})\hat{n}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta^{\alpha}+\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}(\tilde{n}^{\alpha}-\hat{n}^{\alpha})+\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot(\tilde{n}-\hat{n})\hat{n}^{\alpha}.

(4.23)

In light of this, the first term on the RHS of (
4.19
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta^{\alpha})\,dS\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}[\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}(\tilde{n}^{\alpha}-\hat{n}^{\alpha})]\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}[\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot(\tilde{n}-\hat{n})\hat{n}^{\alpha}]\,dS\,dt.

(4.24)

We shall study the main term
I_{00}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta^{\alpha})\,dS\,dt
. The error terms involving
\tilde{n}-\hat{n}
are treated using (
2.22
) and they are identical to the Euler case. We refer
[
11
, (12.16)-(12.19)]
for the details. Invoking (
2.6
)-(
2.7
), we have

\displaystyle I_{00}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\left(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}v^{\lambda}\right)\,dS\,dt

(4.25)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\left(\sqrt{g}(g^{ij}g^{kl}-g^{lj}g^{ik})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta^{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}v^{\lambda}\right).

Integrating
\overline{\partial}{}_{i}
by parts and expanding the parenthesis, we get

\displaystyle\eqref{I000}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}

(4.26)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{g}(g^{ij}g^{kl}-g^{lj}g^{ik})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta^{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta_{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}

\displaystyle-3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda})\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle-3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}\left(\sqrt{g}(g^{ij}g^{kl}-g^{lj}g^{ik})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta^{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta_{\lambda}\right)\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}

\displaystyle-3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{2}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda})\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle-3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{2}\left(\sqrt{g}(g^{ij}g^{kl}-g^{lj}g^{ik})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta^{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta_{\lambda}\right)\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{3}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{3}\left(\sqrt{g}(g^{ij}g^{kl}-g^{lj}g^{ik})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta^{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta_{\lambda}\right)\overline{\partial}{}_{l}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I_{01}+\cdots+I_{08}.

The main terms are
I_{01}
and
I_{02}
which produces the term
|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)|_{0}^{2}
as a part of energy functional, and the others can be controlled by estimating
I_{03}+I_{04},I_{05}+I_{06},I_{07}+I_{08}
and integrating
\partial_{t}
by parts. In
I_{01}
, we integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts and use (
2.4
) in Lemma
2.1
to get

\displaystyle I_{01}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\bigg|^{T}_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda})\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\,dS\,dt

(4.27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}(\Pi^{\alpha}_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}(\Pi^{\mu}_{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda})+\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}(\Pi^{\mu}_{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\Pi^{\mu}_{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda})\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\,dS\,dt+I_{01}|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I_{011}+I_{012}+I_{013}+I_{014}+I_{01}|_{t=0}.

The term
I_{011}
produces the energy term

\displaystyle I_{011}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)\right|^{2}\,dS-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}-\delta^{ij})\overline{\partial}{}_{i}(\Pi^{\alpha}_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}(\Pi^{\mu}_{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda})\,dS

(4.28)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)\right|_{0}^{2}\left|\sqrt{g}g^{ij}-\delta^{ij}\right|_{1.5}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)\right|_{0}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}\eta\|_{2},\|\overline{\partial}{}\eta\|_{2})\,dt.

The terms
I_{012},I_{013},I_{014}
can all be directly controlled. Because
\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta|_{t=0}=0
, then

\displaystyle I_{012}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{g}g^{-1}\right|_{L^{\infty}}|\overline{\partial}{}\Pi|_{L^{\infty}}|\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{0}|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)|_{0}

(4.29)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(|\overline{\partial}{}\eta|_{L^{\infty}},|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta|_{L^{\infty}})\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0.5}|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)\right|_{0}^{2}+P(\|\eta\|_{4})\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)\right|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\eta\|_{4},\|v\|_{4},\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0})\,dt,

and

\displaystyle I_{013}\lesssim

\displaystyle|\overline{\partial}{}\Pi|_{L^{4}}^{2}|\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{L^{4}}^{2}\lesssim P(\|\overline{\partial}{}\eta\|_{2})\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0}\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}

(4.30)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+P(\|\overline{\partial}{}\eta\|_{2})\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}v\|_{2}\,dt,

and

I_{014}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\left|\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v\right|_{0}^{2}\left|\partial_{t}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi)\right|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5},\|v\|_{3},\|\eta\|_{3})\,dt.

(4.31)

Combining (
4.27
) with (
4.28
)-(
4.31
), we get the estimates of
I_{01}
as follows

I_{01}\lesssim\varepsilon\left(\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v)\right|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}\,dt.

(4.32)

Next we control
I_{02}:=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{g}(g^{ij}g^{kl}-g^{lj}g^{ik})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta^{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta_{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}
. We expand the summation on
l,i
and find that:

•

When
l=i
, this integral is zero thanks to the symmetry.

•

When
l=1,i=2
, the integrand becomes
\sqrt{g}^{-1}(\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\alpha}-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\lambda})\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}v^{\alpha}
.

•

When
l=2,i=1
, the integrand becomes
-\sqrt{g}^{-1}(\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\alpha}-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\lambda})\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}v^{\alpha}
.

Here, we use
g^{-1}
to denote
\det[g^{-1}]=g^{11}g^{22}-g^{12}g^{21}
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle I_{02}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\left(\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\alpha}-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\lambda}\right)\left(\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}v^{\alpha}+\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}v^{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}v^{\alpha}\right)\,dS\,dt

(4.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\det\underbrace{\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}v^{\mu}&\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}v^{\mu}\\
\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}v^{\mu}&\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}v^{\mu}\end{bmatrix}}_{=:\mathbf{A}}\right)+\text{ lower order terms}

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{t}}{=}

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\det\mathbf{A}\bigg|^{T}_{0}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\right)\det\mathbf{A}

The first term in the last line of (
4.33
) can be expanded into two terms

\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\det\mathbf{A}=\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\left(\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}\right).

(4.34)

It can be seen that the top order terms cancel with each other if one integrates
\overline{\partial}{}_{1}
by parts in the first term and
\overline{\partial}{}_{2}
by parts in the second. The remaining terms are all of the form
-\int_{\Gamma}Q_{\mu\lambda}(\overline{\partial}{}\eta,\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta)\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}
, which can be controlled as

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma}Q_{\mu\lambda}(\overline{\partial}{}\eta,\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta)\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta|_{L^{\infty}},|\overline{\partial}{}\eta|_{L^{\infty}})|\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{0}|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0.5}\int_{0}^{T}P(\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}v\|_{2})\,dt.

(4.35)

The second term of (
4.33
) can be directly controlled, i.e.,

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\right)\det\mathbf{A}\lesssim|\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}\eta|_{L^{\infty}}|\overline{\partial}{}\eta|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{0}^{2}\,dt\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.36)

Therefore, we get the estimates of
I_{02}
:

I_{02}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}\,dt,

(4.37)

Next we control the remaining terms in
I_{0}
, i.e.,
I_{03},\cdots,I_{08}
. The strategy here is to study
I_{03}+I_{04},I_{05}+I_{06},I_{07}+I_{08}
, where

\displaystyle I_{03}+I_{04}=

\displaystyle-3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}(Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta))\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}v\,dS\,dt

(4.38)

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{t}}{=}

\displaystyle 3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{2}(Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta))\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v+3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}(Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta))\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v+3\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}(Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta))\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v\bigg|_{0}^{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\left(\underbrace{Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta)\overline{\partial}{}v}_{\partial_{t}(Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta))}\overline{\partial}{}v+Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}v\right)\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v

\displaystyle+3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta)\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v+\int_{\Gamma}Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta)\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v\,dS\bigg|_{0}^{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}+|Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta)\overline{\partial}{}v|_{L^{\infty}}|\partial_{t}^{2}v|_{1}|\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Similarly, by plugging
\partial_{t}^{3}(Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta))=Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta)(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}v\overline{\partial}{}v\overline{\partial}{}v+\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}v\overline{\partial}{}v+\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}v)
into
I_{05}+I_{06}
, we get

\displaystyle I_{05}+I_{06}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{2}(Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta))\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{4}\overline{\partial}{}v\,dS\,dt

(4.39)

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{t}}{=}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{3}(Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta))\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v\,dS\,dt-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{2}(Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta))\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v+\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{2}(Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta))\partial_{t}\overline{\partial}{}v\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v\bigg|^{T}_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}+|\overline{\partial}{}v|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}|\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}v|_{0}|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}v|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}v\|_{1.5}^{4}+\|v\|_{3}^{8}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}.

Following the same way as above, we can control
I_{07}+I_{08}
by
\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}
so we omit the details. Combining this with (
4.26
), (
4.32
), (
4.37
)-(
4.39
), we get the estimates of
I_{0}
by

I_{0}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v\right)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.40)

Now the only term left to control in (
4.2
) is
L
. Expanding
[\partial_{t}^{4},\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}]
, we have

\displaystyle L=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q\,dy\,dt+4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q\,dy\,dt

(4.41)

\displaystyle+6\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q\,dy\,dt+4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle L_{21}+L_{22}+L_{23}+L_{24}.

Despite having the right amount of derivatives, there is no direct control of
\|\partial_{t}^{4}q\|_{0}
and so we have to make some extra efforts to control
L_{21},\cdots,L_{24}
.

The hardest term to treat here is
L_{21}
. Since

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}=\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{J}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}=

\displaystyle\tilde{J}(\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}+(\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{J})\underbrace{\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}}_{=0}+\text{lower-order terms}

(4.42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}+\text{lower-order terms}

we have

\displaystyle L_{21}\overset{L}{=}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q.

(4.43)

Since

\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q=\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}q)-(\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha})q-4(\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha})\partial_{t}q-6(\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha})\partial_{t}^{2}q-4(\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha})\partial_{t}^{3}q,

and thus one can write the RHS of (
4.43
) as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}q)-4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{t}q

\displaystyle-6\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{t}^{2}q-4\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\beta}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}q

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle L_{211}+L_{212}+L_{213}+L_{214}.

It is not hard to see that
L_{212},L_{213},L_{214}
can all be controlled directly by
\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
thanks to (
3.36
). To treat
L_{211}
, we integrate
\partial_{\beta}
by parts and get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}q)-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\beta}\Big(\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}q)\Big)=L_{2111}+L_{2112}.

Since
L_{2112}\overset{L}{=}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\beta}(\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}q)
,
we integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts in the last term and get

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{\beta}(\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}q)\bigg|_{0}^{T}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}\Big(\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\Big)\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{\beta}(\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}q)=L_{21121}+L_{21122}.

(4.44)

Now, since
\partial_{\beta}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}=0
, we can write

\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{\beta}(\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}q)=-\partial_{t}^{4}v^{\alpha}+\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta^{\alpha}.

(4.45)

In light of this, we have

\displaystyle L_{21122}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.46)

Also,

\displaystyle L_{21121}=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}(-\partial_{t}^{4}v^{\alpha}+\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta^{\alpha})\bigg|^{T}_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\|\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0}(\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0}+\|b_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\varepsilon(\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1}^{2})+P(\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0},\|\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\|_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\varepsilon(\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1}^{2})+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Moreover, by plugging the boundary condition (
3.6
) to
L_{2111}
we obtain

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha})+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}\Big(\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\tilde{n})\right)\tilde{n}^{\alpha}\Big)=L_{21111}+L_{21112}.

Invoking (
2.6
), we have

\displaystyle L_{21111}=

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha})

\displaystyle+\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}g^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\eta^{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha}).

It suffices to control the first term only since the second term has the highest order contribution with the same type of integrand. Also,

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha})\overset{L}{=}

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}(\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{n}^{\alpha}).

Now, since

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{n}=Q(\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta})\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n}+\text{lower-order terms},

(4.47)

and so we have, after using the Sobolev embedding and trace lemma, that

\displaystyle\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\left|\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}(\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{n}^{\alpha})\right|\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.48)

In addition, by integrating
\overline{\partial}{}_{i}
by parts and then using the trace lemma, we have

\displaystyle\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\left|\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha}\right|\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.49)

Moreover, we still need to control
L_{21112}
. In light of (
4.47
), we only need to study the case when all four time derivatives land on
\overline{\Delta}{}v
, i.e.,

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\overline{\Delta}{}(\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\tilde{n})\tilde{n}^{\alpha}.

Integrating
\overline{\partial}{}
by parts, this term has the contributes to

\displaystyle\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha},

up to terms with the same type integrand, whose analysis (and bound) is identical. To control the main term, one has

\displaystyle\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{v}_{\nu}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha}=\sqrt{\kappa}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}Q(\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta},\partial v)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\kappa}\int_{0}^{T}Q(\|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\sqrt{\kappa}\int_{0}^{T}Q(\|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}},\|\partial v\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\kappa}E_{\kappa}^{(3)}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Finally, combining (
4.1
) with the computations above, we finally get the control of full time derivatives

\left\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left(\Pi\partial_{t}^{3}v\right)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim E_{\kappa}^{(3)}+(E_{\kappa}^{(3)})^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.50)

4.2
Control of mixed space-time tangential derivatives

To finish the control of
E_{\kappa}(T)
, it remains to study the tangential energies generated by the
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}
,
\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}
,
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}
and
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
-differentiated
\kappa
-problem. Generally speaking, the energy estimate becomes much simpler when the tangential spatial derivative(s)
\overline{\partial}{}
is taken into account. This is due to that we can avoid the higher-order terms in the interior, i.e., terms associated with
I_{11}
in (
4.3
). This can be done by having all top-order terms on the boundary, and those terms can be controlled thanks to the extra
0.5
interior regularity.

The
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}
-tangential energy:
Similar to (
4.1
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}\left|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right|^{2}\,dy\,dt

(4.51)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}q)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\,dy\,dt}_{I^{*}}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\,dy\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\alpha\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v_{\alpha}\,dy\,dt.

By integrating
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
by parts in the second term, we can get the cancellation with the third term at the top order

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\,dy\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\alpha\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v_{\alpha}\,dy\,dt

(4.52)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v_{\alpha}\,dy\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v_{\alpha}\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left[\overline{\partial}{},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\right]\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{\alpha}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}-\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{\alpha}\cdot\left[(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\overline{\partial}{}\right]\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\,dy\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}P(\|b_{0}\|_{3},\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2})\,dt

The main term
I^{*}
is treated a bit different compare to
I
in (
4.2
). Specifically, one commutes
\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}
with
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}
first and then integrates by parts. This allows us to avoid the appearance of the higher-order interior terms.

\displaystyle I^{*}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{\mu}q\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}~\left[\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3},\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\right]\partial_{\mu}q}_{L_{1}^{*}}

(4.53)

\displaystyle\overset{\partial_{\mu}}{=}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}q\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}q}_{I_{B}^{*}}\underbrace{+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{0}}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}q}_{I_{B}^{**}}+L_{1}^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\underbrace{\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(\text{div}_{\tilde{A}}v)}_{=0}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}q\underbrace{+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left[\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha},\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\right]\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}~\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}q}_{L_{2}^{*}}+I_{B}^{*}+I_{B}^{**}+L_{1}^{*}.

Here,
I_{B}^{**}=0
because
\tilde{A}^{13}=\tilde{A}^{23}=0
,
\tilde{A}^{33}=1
, and
v_{3}=0
on
\Gamma_{0}
. Also,
L_{1}^{*}
and
L_{2}^{*}
can be directly controlled. For simplicity we only list the computation of the highest order terms

\displaystyle L_{1}^{*}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}~\left[\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3},\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\right]\partial_{\mu}q\,dy\,dt

(4.54)

\displaystyle\overset{L}{=}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}~\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}q\,dy\,dt\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}\,dt.

and

\displaystyle L_{2}^{*}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left[\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha},\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\right]\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}~\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}q\,dy\,dt

(4.55)

\displaystyle\overset{L}{=}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}~\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}q\,dy\,dt\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}\,dt.

Next we analyze the boundary integral
I_{B}^{*}
.

\displaystyle I_{B}^{*}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}q)\,dS\,dt

(4.56)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}q\,dS\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}q\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle+3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}q\,dS\,dt+3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}q\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle+3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{2}q\,dS\,dt+3\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}q\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{3}q\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle J_{0}+J_{1}+\cdots+J_{7}.

Since we have
H^{1.5}(\Omega)
regularity for
\partial_{t}^{3}v
and
H^{1}(\Omega)
regularity for
\partial_{t}^{3}q
, the top order terms contributed by
J_{1}
to
J_{7}
can all be directly controlled by the trace lemma. In the end, we have

J_{1}+\cdots+J_{7}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.57)

By plugging the boundary condition

\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}q=-\sigma\sqrt{g}(\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n})\tilde{n}^{\alpha}+\kappa\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\tilde{n})\right)\tilde{n}^{\alpha}

in
J_{0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma}J_{0}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}\tilde{n}^{\alpha})\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\,dS\,dt-\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}[\left((1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\tilde{n})\right)\tilde{n}^{\alpha}]\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\,dS\,dt

(4.58)

For the second term, after integrating one
\overline{\partial}{}
by parts, it contributes to the positive energy term (after moving to the LHS)

\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}|\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n}|_{2}^{2}\,dS\,dt,

(4.59)

and some error terms. Here, the most difficult error term reads

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt

(4.60)

which can be treated as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt\overset{L}{=}\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{v}\cdot\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}P(|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}},|v|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}})|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{0}|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}v\cdot\tilde{n}|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}v|_{0}^{2}+\sup_{t}P(|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}},|v|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}})+\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}v\cdot\tilde{n}|_{0}^{2}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{2.5}^{2}+\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{3.5}^{2}\Big)^{2}+\sup_{t}P(|\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}},|v|_{L^{\infty}{(\Gamma)}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E_{\kappa}^{(3)}+(E_{\kappa}^{(3)})^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

The first term in (
4.58
) is treated analogous to the first term in (
4.19
). The main term we need to study in this case reads

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta^{\alpha}))(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v)\,dS\,dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\left(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}v^{\lambda}+\sqrt{g}(g^{ij}g^{kl}-g^{lj}g^{ik})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta^{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}v_{\lambda}\right)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{\lambda}\,dS\,dt

Integrating
\overline{\partial}{}_{i}
by parts, we get

\displaystyle J_{00}\overset{\overline{\partial}{}_{i}}{=}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Pi^{\alpha}_{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}v^{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}v_{\alpha}\,dS\,dt

(4.61)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{g}(g^{ij}g^{kl}-g^{lj}g^{ik})\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta^{\alpha}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\partial_{t}^{2}v_{\lambda}\partial_{t}^{3}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}v_{\alpha}\,dS\,dt+\mathcal{R}_{0}

\displaystyle=:J_{01}+J_{02}+\mathcal{R}_{0},

where
\mathcal{R}_{0}
