consists terms that can be treated in the same way as in
I_{03},\cdots,I_{08}
in (
4.26
).

In
J_{01}
, we can integrate
\partial_{t}
by parts and mimic the proof of (
4.27
) to get

J_{01}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}(\Pi\partial_{t}^{2}v)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon\left(\left|\overline{\partial}{}(\Pi\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}v)\right|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2.5}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}\,dt

(4.62)

J_{02}
can also be controlled similarly as
I_{02}
. We find that the integrand is zero if
l=i
. So it suffices to compute the case
(l,i)=(1,2)
and
(2,1)
. Similarly we get

J_{02}=\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\det\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}&\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}\\
\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}&\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}\end{bmatrix}\,dS\bigg|^{T}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}+\mathcal{R}.

(4.63)

The main term can be computed as follows

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\det\begin{bmatrix}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}&\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}\\
\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}&\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}\end{bmatrix}\,dS

(4.64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}\frac{1}{\sqrt{g}}\left(\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}-\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{2}\eta_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{1}\partial_{t}^{2}\overline{\partial}{}v^{\mu}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\overline{\partial}{}_{1},\overline{\partial}{}_{2}}{=}

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma}Q_{\mu\lambda}^{i}(\overline{\partial}{}\eta,\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta)\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}v^{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}v^{\lambda}\,dS

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P\left(|\overline{\partial}{}\eta|_{L^{\infty}},|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta|_{L^{\infty}}\right)|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}v|_{0}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}v|_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2.5}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

and thus we get the control of
J_{02}

J_{02}\lesssim\varepsilon\left(\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2.5}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.65)

Combining (
4.51
)-(
4.62
) and (
4.65
), we get the
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}
-tangential estimates as follows

\left\|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left(\Pi\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{2}v\right)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim E_{\kappa}^{(3)}+(E_{\kappa}^{(3)})^{2}+\varepsilon\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\|_{2.5}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.66)

The
\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}
,
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}
and
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
-tangential energies:
The control of the other tangential energies that involving at least one
\overline{\partial}{}
is follows from the arguments above by replacing
\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}
to the corresponding derivatives. Hence, we shall omit the details and only illustrate the major differences.

First, we mention that the derivatives
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}
and
\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
behave the same since both
v
and
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
are of the same interior regularity.

Second, one needs to pay attention to the terms that analogous to the error term generated by (
4.58
) during the construction of the energy term. In particular, we need to study the top order error term analogous to (
4.60
). Setting
\mathfrak{D}=\partial_{t},\overline{\partial}{}
or
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
, and so
\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2},\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t},\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
can be denoted systematically by
\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\mathfrak{D}^{2}
. Now we consider

\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\mathfrak{D}^{2}v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\mathfrak{D}^{2}\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt.

(4.67)

When
\mathfrak{D}^{2}=\partial_{t}^{2}
then (
4.67
) is treated similar to (
4.60
). This is due to that

\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{n}=Q(\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta})\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}\tilde{v}\cdot\tilde{n}+\text{lower-order terms},

and
\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{t}^{4}v|_{1.5}^{2}
is included in
E_{\kappa}^{(3)}
.
In the end, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}^{2}\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt\leq E_{\kappa}^{(3)}+(E_{\kappa}^{(3)})^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

On the other hand, when
\mathfrak{D}^{2}=\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t},\overline{\partial}{}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
, then using the fact that
\mathfrak{D}\tilde{n}=Q(\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta})\mathfrak{D}\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{\eta}\cdot\tilde{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt\overset{L}{=}\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{4}\partial_{t}v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{5}v\cdot\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt,

(4.68)

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt\overset{L}{=}\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v\cdot\tilde{n})(v\cdot\overline{\partial}{}^{5}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}\cdot\tilde{n})\,dS\,dt.

(4.69)

The terms on the RHS requires
\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}v|_{5}^{2}
and
\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta|_{5}^{2}
, respectively, to control. However, owing to (
3.30
) and (
3.32
), both of them can be controlled by

\mathcal{M}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}v\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left(\Pi\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}v\right)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim E_{\kappa}^{(3)}+(E_{\kappa}^{(3)})^{2}+\varepsilon E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(4.70)

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}v\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left(\Pi\overline{\partial}{}^{3}v\right)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim E_{\kappa}^{(3)}+(E_{\kappa}^{(3)})^{2}+\varepsilon E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{M}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(4.71)

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left\|\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta\right\|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left(\Pi\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right|_{0}^{2}\lesssim E_{\kappa}^{(3)}+(E_{\kappa}^{(3)})^{2}+\varepsilon E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{M}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(4.72)

Notice that the RHS of (
4.71
) and (
4.72
) rely on
\mathcal{M}_{0}
, which is given in Lemma
3.5
. In Section
6
, in fact, we are able to control
\mathcal{M}_{0}
by
\mathcal{C}(\|v_{0}\|_{4.5},\|b_{0}\|_{4.5},|v_{0}|_{5})
.

5
Estimates for the higher order weighted interior norms

It remains to control
E_{\kappa}^{(3)}(T)
in order to complete the proof of Proposition
3.1
.

5.1
Full time derivatives

We shall first study the first two terms, i.e.,

\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\big\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\big\|_{1.5}^{2}+\big\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\big\|_{1.5}^{2}\Big)\,dt=K_{1}+K_{2}.

These terms appear to be the most difficult ones to control. In particular, they yield error terms that contribute to the top order and can only be controlled in
L^{2}(0,T)
instead of
L^{\infty}(0,T)
. In other words, we cannot use the time integral to create terms that can be controlled by
\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
.

The goal is to show:

\displaystyle K_{1}+K_{2}\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(5.1)

The control of
K_{1},K_{2}
relies on the div-curl estimate and so the
H^{1}
-norms of
\partial_{t}^{4}v
and
\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
have to be studied together owing to the strong coupling structure of the MHD equations. In particular,

\displaystyle K_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left(\big\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,\partial_{t}^{4}v\big\|_{0.5}^{2}+\big\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{4}v\big\|_{0.5}^{2}+\big|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v^{3}\big|_{1}^{2}\right)\,dt=:K_{11}+K_{12}+K_{13},

(5.2)

\displaystyle K_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left(\big\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\big\|_{0.5}^{2}+\big\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\big\|_{0.5}^{2}+\big|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}\big|_{1}^{2}\right)\,dt=:K_{21}+K_{22}+K_{23}.

(5.3)

Bound for
K_{13}
and
K_{23}
:

For
K_{13}
, there holds

\displaystyle K_{13}\leq\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\tilde{n}|_{1}^{2}}_{\leq E_{\kappa}^{(2)}}+\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot(N-\tilde{n})|_{1}^{2},

and for the error term, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot(N-\tilde{n})|_{1}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}\cdot|N-\tilde{n}|_{1+}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon^{2}\mathcal{P},

where (
3.21
) is used in the last inequality. To control
K_{23}
, since
\partial_{t}\eta=v
we have

\int_{0}^{T}\big|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v^{3}\big|_{1}^{2}

and so it suffices to control

\int_{0}^{T}\big|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}v^{3}\big|_{2}^{2}.

This term can then be treated similarly to
K_{13}
.

Bound for
K_{11}
and
K_{21}
:
First we state the following application of the Kato-Ponce inequality which shall be used frequently. Let
f\in H^{0.5}(\Omega)
and
g
be a smooth function. Then

\displaystyle\|fg\|_{0.5}\lesssim\|f\|_{0.5}\|g\|_{1.5+}.

(5.4)

For
K_{11}
, we have

\displaystyle K_{11}\leq\int_{0}^{T}(\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,_{a-\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}^{2}).

(5.5)

Since
\|a-\tilde{a}\|_{1.5+}^{2}\leq\kappa P(\|\eta\|_{3.5})
thanks to (
3.26
), the error term can be controlled as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,_{a-\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|a-\tilde{a}\|_{1.5+}^{2}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}^{2},

(5.6)

which can be controlled by the RHS of (
5.1
) when
\kappa
is small.
For the first term, since
\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}v=0

we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}^{2}=\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}[\partial_{t}^{4},\tilde{a}]\partial v\|_{0.5}^{2}\overset{L}{=}\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\alpha}\|_{0.5}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\|_{0.5}^{2}.

(5.7)

It is not hard to see that that
\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}\tilde{a}\partial\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{0.5}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
as
\partial_{t}^{3}v\in H^{1.5}(\Omega)
a priori. In addition, since

\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}=Q(\partial\tilde{\eta}),

we obtain

\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}\tilde{a}\partial v\|_{0.5}^{2}\overset{L}{=}\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}Q(\partial\tilde{\eta})\partial\partial_{t}^{3}v\partial v\|_{0.5}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(5.8)

The control of
K_{21}
is a bit more involved. We cannot commute
\partial_{t}^{4}
to (
3.67
) as this would yield
\text{div }_{\partial_{t}^{5}\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
on the RHS which cannot be controlled. However, by writing
\text{div}\,\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta=\text{div}\,\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v
and then we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v\|_{0.5}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v\|_{0.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,_{\tilde{a}-a}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v\|_{0.5}^{2}.

(5.9)

The second term on the RHS is again easy to control similar to (
5.6
). For the first term, because

\displaystyle\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{3}\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)v)-[\partial_{t}^{3},\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}](b_{0}\cdot\partial)v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{3}([\text{div}_{\tilde{a}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]v)-[\partial_{t}^{3},\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}](b_{0}\cdot\partial)v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}(\partial_{t}^{i}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})(\partial_{\mu}b_{0}^{\nu})(\partial_{t}^{3-i}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha})-\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\partial_{t}^{i}\left(b_{0}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\right)(\partial_{t}^{3-i}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha})

\displaystyle-\sum_{1\leq j\leq 3}(\partial_{t}^{j}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3-j}v_{\alpha}),

then it can be seen, after counting the derivatives that

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\partial_{t}^{i}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})(\partial_{\mu}b_{0}^{\nu})(\partial_{t}^{3-i}\partial_{\nu}v_{\alpha})\|_{0.5}^{2},\quad\sum_{0\leq i\leq 3}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{i}\left(b_{0}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\right)(\partial_{t}^{3-i}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha})\|_{0.5}^{2},

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq 3}\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\partial_{t}^{j}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{3-j}v_{\alpha})\|_{0.5}^{2}

can be controlled by
\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
owing to the fact that
\partial_{t}^{k}v\in H^{4.5-k}(\Omega)
,
k=2,3
.

Bound for
K_{12}
and
K_{22}
:
We would like to state the following strategy that will come in handy when dealing with the leading order terms in
K_{12}
and
K_{22}
. Let
X
be the term such that
\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}X\|_{0.5}^{2}
is part of
E_{\kappa}^{(3)}
and
Y
be a lower order term such that
\|Y\|_{1.5+}^{2}
is controlled by
E_{\kappa}^{(1)}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}XY\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt\leq

\displaystyle T\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}XY\|_{0.5}^{2}\lesssim T\sup_{t}\|Y\|_{1.5+}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}X\|_{0.5}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}X\|_{0.5}^{2}\Big)^{2}+\frac{T^{2}}{2\varepsilon}\sup_{t}\|Y\|_{1.5+}^{4},

(5.10)

which is bounded by the RHS of (
3.13
) if
T
is sufficiently small.

K_{12}
and
K_{22}
will be considered together via studying the evolution equation verified by
\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{4}v
and
\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
. But this evolution equation cannot be derived by taking
\partial_{t}^{4}
to (
3.75
) as this would yield
\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}^{5}\tilde{A}}v
in the source term which cannot be controlled. Instead, we commute
\partial_{t}^{4}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}
to the equation

\partial_{t}v+(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta=\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q

and get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}^{4}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}v+\partial_{t}^{4}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta)=0.

This yields the following evolution equation by commuting three time derivatives through
\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}
in the first term on the LHS:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v+\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta)=-\partial_{t}([\partial_{t}^{3},\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}]\partial_{t}v)-[\partial_{t}^{4},\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}](b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta:=f,

(5.11)

and, after expansion, the source term
f
becomes:

\displaystyle f=\partial_{t}\bigg(\sum_{1\leq j\leq 3}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{t}^{j}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta})\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4-j}v^{\gamma}\bigg)+\sum_{1\leq j\leq 4}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{t}^{j}\tilde{a}^{\mu\beta})\partial_{\mu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{4-j}\eta^{\gamma}.

(5.12)

By multiplying
\kappa\overline{\partial}{}(\text{curl }_{\tilde{a}}\partial_{t}^{4}v)
to the evolution equation (
5.11
) and then integrating in space, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\kappa\partial_{t}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v)\overline{\partial}{}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v)+\int_{\Omega}\kappa\Big(\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\Big)\overline{\partial}{}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v)=\int_{\Omega}\kappa f\overline{\partial}{}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v),

(5.13)

where the first term contributes to
\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}^{2}
after integrating
\overline{\partial}{}^{\frac{1}{2}}
by parts.

Next, if we integrate
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
by parts in
\int_{\Omega}\kappa\Big(\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\Big)\overline{\partial}{}(\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v)
and then integrate
\overline{\partial}{}^{\frac{1}{2}}
by parts, we obtain
\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0.5}^{2}
up to terms involving commutators (which will be recorded below). In particular, the following energy inequality is achieved:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0.5}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}f\|_{0.5}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}\,dt

(5.14)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}[\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)](b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\|_{0.5}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}[\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\|_{0.5}\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0.5}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\|_{0.5}\,dt.

(5.15)

Hence, by integrating in time one more time, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0.5}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}f\|_{0.5}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}[\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)](b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\|_{0.5}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}[\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\|_{0.5}\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0.5}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\|_{0.5}\,dt,

(5.16)

where we have dropped one
\,dt
for the sake of concise notations.
This suggests that we should control

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}f\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt,\quad\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}[\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)](b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}[\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt,\quad\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\tilde{A}}\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt.

For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}[\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)](b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\tilde{A}^{\mu\beta}(\partial_{\mu}b_{0}^{\nu})(\partial_{\nu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta^{\gamma}\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(b_{0}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}\tilde{A}^{\mu\beta})(\partial_{\mu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\partial_{t}^{4}\eta^{\gamma}\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt,

which can be controlled by the RHS of (
5.1
) by adapting (
5.10
).
The third and forth term are treated analogously. For
\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}f\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt
, invoking (
5.12
), we need to consider

\displaystyle i=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq 3}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}\big(\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{t}^{j}\tilde{A}^{\mu\beta})\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4-j}v^{\gamma}\big)\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt,

(5.17)

\displaystyle ii=

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j\leq 4}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{t}^{j}\tilde{A}^{\mu\beta})\partial_{\mu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{4-j}\eta^{\gamma}\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt.

(5.18)

Here,
i\overset{L}{=}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\partial_{t}\tilde{A})(\partial\partial_{t}^{4}v)\|_{0.5}^{2}
, which controlled appropriately by adapting (
5.10
). Moreover,

\displaystyle ii\overset{L}{=}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{\mu\beta})\partial_{\mu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}\eta^{\gamma}\|_{0.5}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\partial_{t}\tilde{A})\partial\partial_{t}^{3}[(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta]\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(5.19)

since
\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{2.5}^{2}
is included in
E_{\kappa}^{(3)}
, and this concludes the control of
K_{1}+K_{2}
.

Remark
.

There is an alternative way to control the last integral in (
5.19
). We may use the equation to replace
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta
by
\partial_{t}v+\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q
, and this allow us to control this integral without using
\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{2.5}^{2}
. In fact, one can show

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}q\|_{2.5}^{2}\,dt\leq\mathcal{P}

by employing the elliptic estimate we used in Section
3.1
(similar to the control of (
5.24
)),
and so

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\partial_{t}\tilde{A})\partial\partial_{t}^{3}[(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta]\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\partial_{t}\tilde{A})\partial\partial_{t}^{4}v\|^{2}_{0.5}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\partial_{t}\tilde{A})\partial\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

because
\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}
is part of
E_{\kappa}^{(3)}
.

5.2
Mixed space-time derivatives

The treatment for the remaining terms of
E_{\kappa}^{(3)}
is parallel and so we shall only sketch the details. We shall consider

\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\big\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{k}v\big\|_{5.5-k}^{2}+\big\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\big\|_{5.5-k}^{2}\Big)\,dt,\quad k=1,2,3.

First, the boundary terms contributed by the time derivative(s) of
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
, i.e., terms analogous to
K_{23}
, reads

\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{5-k}^{2},\quad k=1,2,3.

Generally speaking, for each fixed
k=1,2,3
, the control of the above term requires that of
\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{k-1}v^{3}|_{6-k}^{2}
, and this process stops when
k=1
. In particular,
for each fixed
k=2,3
, we write
\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{5-k}^{2}
as
\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{k-1}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v^{3}|_{5-k}^{2}
, which can then be controlled together with
\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{j}v^{3}|_{5-j}^{2}
with
j=1,2
. On the other hand, when
k=1
, the control of
\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{4}^{2}
requires that of

\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v^{3}|_{4}^{2}\lesssim P(\|b_{0}\|_{4.5})\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}v^{3}|_{5}^{2},

where, in view of (
3.31
), we have
\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}v|_{5}^{2}\leq\mathcal{M}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
.

Second, the control of the analogous terms of
ii
(defined in (
5.18
)) for
k=1,2,3
requires a similar analysis as above. For each fixed
k
, we need to investigate

\displaystyle ii^{\prime}=\sum_{1\leq j\leq k}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{t}^{j}\tilde{A}^{\mu\beta})\partial_{\mu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{k-j}\eta^{\gamma}\|_{4.5-k}^{2}\,dt.

(5.20)

Again, it suffices to consider the most difficult term contributed by setting
j=1
, i.e.,

\displaystyle ii^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{\mu\beta})\partial_{\mu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial_{t}^{k-1}\eta^{\gamma}\|_{4.5-k}^{2}\,dt

(5.21)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}P(\|v\|_{4.5},\|b_{0}\|_{4.5},\|\eta\|_{4.5})\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial^{3}\partial_{t}^{k-1}\eta\|_{4.5-k}^{2}\,dt.

(5.22)

In (
5.22
), it can be seen that when
k=2,3
,
\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial^{3}\partial_{t}^{k-1}\eta\|_{4.5-k}\,dt
is bounded by
\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial^{3}v\|_{2.5}^{2}\,dt
and
\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial^{3}\partial_{t}v\|_{1.5}^{2}\,dt
, respectively. Moreover, when
k=1
, we need to consider (
5.21
) instead. The strategy here is to replace
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta
by
\partial_{t}v+\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q
, and so

\displaystyle ii^{\prime}=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{\mu\beta})\partial_{\mu}\partial_{t}v^{\gamma}\|_{3.5}^{2}\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{\mu\beta})\partial_{\mu}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}^{\gamma}q\|_{3.5}^{2}\,dt,

(5.23)

where the first term is bounded by the RHS of (
5.1
) owing to (
5.10
). For the second term, since
v\in H^{4.5}(\Omega)
, so it suffices to consider the case when all derivatives land on
\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q
, whose control requires that of

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{4.5}^{2}\,dt

(5.24)

after adapting (
5.10
). Actually, we are able to prove a slightly stronger bound by removing one time integral, i.e., we want to bound
\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{4.5}^{2}
. By the div-curl estimate, one has

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{4.5}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{3.5}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{3.5}^{2}+|\sqrt{\kappa}N\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\big|_{3}^{2}+|\sqrt{\kappa}q|_{0}^{2}\Big).

Here,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{3.5}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\Delta_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{3.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,_{\tilde{A}-\delta}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{3.5}^{2},

(5.25)

and by invoking (
3.19
), (
3.36
), (
5.10
), and since
v\in H^{4.5}(\Omega)
a priori, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,_{\tilde{A}-\delta}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{3.5}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{4.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Similarly, because
\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q=0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{3.5}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{4.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Moreover, invoking (
3.21
), (
5.10
) and the trace lemma, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}N\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q|_{3}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\tilde{n}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q|_{3}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}(N-\tilde{n})\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q|_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\tilde{n}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q|_{3}^{2}+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{4.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

As a consequence, (
5.25
) becomes

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{4.5}^{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\bigg(\|\sqrt{\kappa}\Delta_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{3.5}^{2}+|\sqrt{\kappa}\tilde{n}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q\big|_{3}^{2}+|\sqrt{\kappa}q|_{0}^{2}\bigg).

(5.26)

To control the RHS, we recall that
q
verifies

-\Delta_{\tilde{A}}q=-\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}+\partial_{\beta}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\tilde{\eta}_{\nu})\partial_{\nu}\tilde{A}^{\mu\nu}\tilde{A}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{\mu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}

(5.27)

with the Dirichlet and Neumann boundary conditions

\displaystyle\sqrt{\tilde{g}}q=

\displaystyle-\sigma\sqrt{g}(\Delta_{g}\eta\cdot\tilde{n})+\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\tilde{n}),

(5.28)

\displaystyle\tilde{n}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q=

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}v\cdot\tilde{n}+(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}.

(5.29)

Now,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\Delta_{\tilde{A}}q\|_{3.5}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\kappa\|\partial_{t}\tilde{A}\partial v\|_{3.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\kappa\|\partial((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)(\partial_{t}a)(a\partial(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\|_{3.5}^{2},

(5.30)

and because
v,(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\in H^{4.5}(\Omega)
a priori, the RHS is bounded by
\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
. Also, it is not hard to see, via the trace lemma and the Dirichlet boundary condition, that

\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}q|_{0}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Next, we control
\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\tilde{n}\cdot\nabla_{\tilde{A}}q|_{3}^{2}
. In view of the Neumann boundary condition (
5.29
), it contributes to

\int_{0}^{T}\kappa|\partial_{t}v\cdot\tilde{n}|_{3}^{2},\quad\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}|_{3}^{2}.

For the first term, since
\partial_{t}v\in H^{3.5}(\Omega)
and
\eta\in H^{4.5}(\Omega)
a priori, as well as
\overline{\partial}{}\tilde{n}=Q(\overline{\partial}{}\eta)\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\eta
, we have, after employing the trace theorem, that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\kappa|\partial_{t}v\cdot\tilde{n}|_{3}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

Also, for the second term,

\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}|_{3}^{2}\overset{L}{=}\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\partial^{3}\eta\cdot\tilde{n}|_{0}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}P(\|b_{0}\|_{4.5},||\eta||_{4.5})|\sqrt{\kappa}\overline{\partial}{}^{5}\eta|_{0}^{2},

which can be controlled by
\mathcal{M}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
owing to (
3.30
).

5.3
Full spatial derivatives

It remains for us to bound

\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\big\|\sqrt{\kappa}v\big\|_{5.5}^{2}+\big\|\sqrt{\kappa}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\big\|_{5.5}^{2}\Big)\,dt

in order to conclude the control of
E_{\kappa}^{(3)}(T)
. This will be treated via the div-curl decomposition, and
since there is no time derivative, the arguments are similar to those in Section 3.2.
First, we assume that the quantity

\|\sqrt{\kappa}\eta\|_{5.5}

(5.31)

is bounded a priori.
Second, for the divergence part of velocity, the perturbation argument yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,v\|_{4.5}^{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}-\delta^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\|_{4.5}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}(\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\tilde{a}^{\mu\alpha}\,ds)\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\|_{4.5}^{2}\leq P\left(\|\sqrt{\kappa}\eta\|_{5.5},\|\sqrt{\kappa}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{y}^{5.5}}\right)\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(5.32)

Also, for the divergence part of magnetic field, it suffices to study
\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{div}\,_{\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5}^{2}
by invoking a perturbation argument similar to (
5.3
). We differentiate (
3.67
) with
\sqrt{\kappa}\partial^{4.5}
and then integrate in time to get

\sqrt{\kappa}\partial^{4.5}(\text{div}_{\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)=\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{\kappa}\partial^{4.5}(\partial_{\beta}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}-\tilde{a}^{\mu\gamma}\partial_{\beta}\tilde{v}_{\gamma}\tilde{a}^{\beta\alpha}\partial_{\mu}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}),

and taking
L_{t}^{2}L_{y}^{2}
norm yields that

\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial^{4.5}(\text{div}\,_{\tilde{a}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{y}^{2}}^{2}\lesssim T\left(\|\sqrt{\kappa}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{y}^{5.5}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{y}^{5.5}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{y}^{5.5}}^{2}\right)\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(5.33)

Third, for the curl part of both velocity and magnetic field, it suffices to study
\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}v\|_{4.5}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5}^{2}
by differentiating (
3.75
) with
\partial^{4.5}
and test it with
\kappa\partial^{4.5}\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}}v
in
L_{t}^{2}L_{y}^{2}
. We can show, parallel to (
3.77
), that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left(\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial^{4.5}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{y}^{2}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial^{4.5}\text{curl}_{\tilde{A}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{y}^{2}}^{2}\right)

(5.34)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle P(\|\sqrt{\kappa}b_{0}\|_{5.5},\|\sqrt{\kappa}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{y}^{5.5}},\|\sqrt{\kappa}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{y}^{5.5}},\|\sqrt{\kappa}\eta\|_{5.5}),

Since
\|\sqrt{\kappa}\eta\|_{5.5}\leq\|\sqrt{\kappa}\eta_{0}\|_{5.5}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}v\|_{5.5}
, by the above estimates together with (
3.34
) and (
3.35
) obtained in the discussion of Lemma
3.5
, we get

\|\sqrt{\kappa}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{y}^{5.5}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{y}^{5.5}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}\eta\|_{5.5}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(5.35)

In summary, we have

\displaystyle E_{\kappa}^{(3)}\leq\mathcal{M}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(5.36)

Remark
.

The estimate (
5.35
), together with the trace lemma concludes the proof of Lemma
3.5
.

6
Closing the nonlinear energy estimate

In this section we conclude the proof of Proposition
3.1
.

6.1
Regularity of initial data

Our first task is to remove the extra regularity assumptions on the initial data. These additional regularities are introduced in
\mathcal{M}_{0}
(defined in Lemma
3.5
). In addition to this, one has to control
\|q(0)\|_{4.5},\|q_{t}(0)\|_{3.5},\|q_{tt}(0)\|_{2.5}
in terms of
v_{0}
and
b_{0}
by the elliptic estimate, and extra regularity on
v_{0}
and
b_{0}
shall appear due to the viscosity term.

Note that
q_{0}
verifies the elliptic equation

\begin{cases}-\Delta q_{0}=(\partial v_{0})(\partial v_{0})-(\partial b_{0})(\partial b_{0})~~~&\text{ in }\Omega\\
q_{0}=\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})v^{3}~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma\\
\frac{\partial q_{0}}{\partial N}=0~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma_{0}\\
\end{cases}

(6.1)

by standard elliptic estimates, we get

\|q_{0}\|_{4.5}\lesssim\|\partial v_{0}\|_{2.5}^{2}+\|\partial b_{0}\|_{2.5}^{2}+\kappa\|v_{0}^{3}\|_{4.5}+\kappa|v_{0}^{3}|_{6}.

Moreover, note that the energy functional contains time derivatives of
v
and
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
, so we need to express their initial data in terms of
v_{0}
and
b_{0}
as well. We invoke
\partial_{t}v(0)-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)b_{0}=-\partial q_{0}
to get

\|\partial_{t}v(0)\|_{3.5}\lesssim\|b_{0}\|_{3.5}\|b_{0}\|_{4.5}+\|q_{0}\|_{4.5},

and

\|\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta(0)\|_{3.5}\lesssim\|b_{0}\|_{3.5}\|v_{0}\|_{4.5}.

Similarly, we consider the
\partial_{t}
-differentiated elliptic equation of
q
to get

\|\partial_{t}q(0)\|_{3.5}\lesssim P(\|v_{0}\|_{4.5},\|b_{0}\|_{4.5})(|v_{0}^{3}|_{5}+\kappa|\partial_{t}v(0)|_{5}),

and further

\|\partial_{t}^{2}q(0)\|_{2.5}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}q(0)\|_{1}\lesssim P(\|v_{0}\|_{4.5},\|b_{0}\|_{4.5},|v_{0}|_{5})(1+\kappa|\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{t}^{2}v(0)|_{2}).

By Sobolev trace lemma, we need to bound
\kappa\|\partial_{t}^{2}v(0)\|_{4.5}
which requires the control of
\kappa(\|v_{0}\|_{6.5}+\|b_{0}\|_{5.5}+\|\partial_{t}q(0)\|_{5.5})
. We replace 3.5 by 5.5 in the estimates of
\partial_{t}q(0)
, and thus we need to control

\kappa^{2}|\partial_{t}v(0)|_{7}\lesssim\kappa^{2}(\|b_{0}\|_{7.5}\|b_{0}\|_{8.5}+\|q_{0}\|_{8.5}).

Finally, replacing 4.5 by 8.5 in the estimates of
q_{0}
, we need to control

\kappa^{2}(\|v_{0}\|_{7.5}^{2}+\|b_{0}\|_{7.5}^{2})+\kappa^{3}(|v_{0}|_{8}+|v_{0}|_{10}).

In view of the above analysis and the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{0}
, we need to control
\kappa
-weighted norms of
\|v_{0}\|_{8.5},\|b_{0}\|_{8.5}
and
|v_{0}|_{10}
. However, our given initial data is
v_{0}\in H^{4.5}(\Omega)\cap H^{5}(\Gamma)
and
b_{0}\in H^{4.5}
and so we have to remove the additional regularity assumptions on the initial data. This can be done by adapting a similar argument in Section 12 of Coutand-Shkoller
[
11
]
. We define
\Omega_{\kappa}
to be the regularized version of
\Omega
tangentially mollified by
\zeta_{\exp^{-\kappa}}
and define
E_{\Omega_{\kappa}}
to be the extension operator from
\Omega
to
\Omega_{\kappa}
. Next we set

\textbf{v}_{0}:=\zeta_{\exp^{-\kappa}}*E_{\Omega_{\kappa}}(v_{0}),~\textbf{b}_{0}:=\zeta_{\exp^{-\kappa}}*E_{\Omega_{\kappa}}(b_{0}),~\textbf{q}_{0}:=\zeta_{\exp^{-\kappa}}*E_{\Omega_{\kappa}}(q_{0}).

Therefore, integrating by parts repeatedly to transfer derivatives to the mollifier
\zeta_{\exp^{-\kappa}}
, we get

\|\kappa\textbf{v}_{0}\|_{8.5}+\|\kappa\textbf{b}_{0}\|_{8.5}+\|\kappa\textbf{q}_{0}\|_{8.5}+|\kappa\textbf{v}_{0}|_{10}\lesssim\|v_{0}\|_{4.5}+\|b_{0}\|_{4.5}+\|q_{0}\|_{4.5}+|v_{0}|_{5}\leq\mathcal{C},

(6.2)

where
\mathcal{C}
is the constant that appears in (
3.7
).

6.2
Nonlinear a priori estimates

Now we summarize the a priori estimates of the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation system (
3.2
).

1.

(
3.36
) gives the elliptic estimates of
q
and its time derivatives.

2.

(
3.64
)-(
3.66
) and (
3.73
), (
3.74
) give the divergence estimate and (
3.77
)-(
3.78
) give the curl estimate.

3.

(
3.79
) and (
3.86
) control the boundary part of
v,(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
and its time derivative.

4.

(
4.50
), (
4.66
), (
4.70
)-(
4.72
) provide control of the mixed tangential derivatives of
v
and
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
and the Eulerian normal projections of
v
. Note that these estimate depends on
E_{\kappa}^{(3)}
on the RHS.

5.

Finally, (
5.36
) provides the estimate for
E_{\kappa}^{(3)}
.

Thus, by combining these estimates and then invoking (
6.2
), we obtain a Gronwall-type inequality:

E_{\kappa}(T)-E_{\kappa}(0)\lesssim C(\varepsilon)E_{\kappa}(T)+\mathcal{C}(\|v_{0}\|_{4.5},\|b_{0}\|_{4.5})+P(E_{\kappa}(T))\int_{0}^{T}E_{\kappa}(t)\,dt.

(6.3)

We pick
\varepsilon>0
suitably small such that the
\varepsilon
-terms can be absorbed to LHS. Therefore, by the nonlinear Gronwall inequality in Chapter 2 of Tao
[
53
]
, we know there exists some time
T>0
independent of
\kappa
, such that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}E_{\kappa}(t)\leq\mathcal{C}.

(6.4)

This concludes the proof for Proposition
3.1
.

7
Existence and uniqueness for the linearized approximate system

Since we have obtained a uniform-in-
\kappa
a priori energy estimate for the approximate
\kappa
-problem (
3.2
), our next goal is to construct a solution for this system for each fixed
\kappa>0
that is sufficiently small. We shall assume that
0<\kappa\ll 1

is fixed
throughout the rest of this manuscript.

Let
T>0
. We define

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}=\{\mathbf{u}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{4.5}{(\Omega)}):\sup_{[0,T]}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{4.5}\leq 2(\|v_{0}\|_{4.5}+\|b_{0}\|_{4.5})+1\},

(7.1)

which is a closed subset of the space
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{4.5}(\Omega))
. In order to solve the approximate
\kappa
-problem (
3.2
) for each fixed
\kappa>0
, we study the following linearized problem whose fixed-point shall provide the desired solutions. Fix an arbitrary function
\mathring{\eta}=\mathring{\eta}(t,y)\in\mathbf{X}
whose time derivative
\mathring{\eta}_{t}\in\mathbf{X}
, we denote by
\mathring{a}
,
\mathring{g}
,
\mathring{J}
and
\mathring{A}
the associated quantities in Lagrangian coordinates. Also, we define
\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}
by

\begin{cases}-\Delta\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}=-\Delta\mathring{\eta},~~~&\text{in}\,\,\Omega,\\
\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}=\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\mathring{\eta}~~~&\text{ on }\partial\Omega,\end{cases}

and let
\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}:=[\partial\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}]^{-1},~\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}:=\det[\partial\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}],~\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}:=\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}\mathring{\tilde{{a}}}
and
\mathring{\tilde{n}}
to be the associated smoothed quantities.

We aim to construct
\eta
and
v
that solve

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\eta=v~~~&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
\partial_{t}v-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta+\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}q=0~~~&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
\text{div}\,_{\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}v=0,\quad\text{div }b_{0}=0&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
v^{3}=b_{0}^{3}=0~~~&\text{on}~\Gamma_{0};\\
\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{3\alpha}q=-\sigma\sqrt{\mathring{g}}(\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\mathring{\tilde{n}}^{\alpha}+\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\mathring{\tilde{n}}^{\alpha}~~~&\text{on}~\Gamma;\\
(\eta,v)=(Id,v_{0})~~~&\text{on}~\{t=0\}{\times}\overline{\Omega}.\end{cases}

(7.2)

The rest of this section is devoted to showing the existence of
(\eta,v)
by first establishing the existence of the weak solution and then boosting up their regularity. The construction of the solution for the nonlinear
\kappa
-problem will be postponed until the next section.

We will adapt the method developed in Coutand-Shkoller
[
11
]
to construct the weak solution for (
7.2
). In particular, we study the penalized
\lambda
-problem (
7.4
) whose solution
(\xi_{\lambda},w_{\lambda},q_{\lambda})
can be obtained by the Galerkin’s approximation. Also, we show that this solution converges to that of (
7.2
) when
\lambda\to 0
.
We mention here that in
[
11
]
, the authors were able to prove
w_{\lambda}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))
, which allowed them to obtain a strong solution of the incompressible Euler equations after taking the limit. However, the coupling between the velocity and magnetic field prevents us from employing this technique, as we cannot boost the regularity of
w_{\lambda}
alone without considering
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{\lambda}
.
Because of this, it appears that we have to first construct the weak solution of (
7.2
) in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{-1}(\Omega))
(Section
7.1
), and then prove that this solution in fact has
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))
regularity by employing a bootstrap argument (Subsection
7.2.2
).

Remark
.

There is another reason that prevents us from improving the regularity of
(w_{\lambda},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{\lambda})
. Unlike
w_{\lambda}
, the energy estimate (
7.18
) fails to give any control of the normal component of
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{\lambda}
on the boundary. This is, however, required for estimating
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{\lambda}
in
H^{1}(\Omega)
.

7.1
The penalized problem

The goal of this subsection is to study the penalized version (of the divergence-free condition on the velocity) of the linearized
\kappa
-problem (
7.2
). In particular, for
0<\lambda\ll 1
, let
w_{\lambda},\xi_{\lambda}
be the solutions for (
7.2
) with

\displaystyle\text{div}\,_{\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}w_{\lambda}=-\lambda q_{\lambda},

(7.3)

where
q_{\lambda}
is defined to be the penalized pressure. In this case, (
7.2
) becomes

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\xi_{\lambda}=w_{\lambda}~~~&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
\partial_{t}w_{\lambda}-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{\lambda}+\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}q_{\lambda}=0~~~&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
\text{div}\,_{\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}w_{\lambda}=-\lambda q_{\lambda},\quad\text{div }b_{0}=0&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
w_{\lambda}^{3}=b_{0}^{3}=0~~~&\text{on}~\Gamma_{0};\\
\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{3\alpha}q_{\lambda}=-\sigma\sqrt{\mathring{g}}(\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\mathring{\tilde{n}}^{\alpha}+\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(w_{\lambda}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\mathring{\tilde{n}}^{\alpha}~~~&\text{on}~\Gamma;\\
(\xi_{\lambda},w_{\lambda})=(Id,v_{0})~~~&\text{on}~\{t=0\}{\times}\overline{\Omega}.\end{cases}

(7.4)

Since each penalized problem is indexed by
\lambda
(recall
\kappa
is fixed), we shall denote them by “
\lambda
-problem” throughout the rest of this section.

7.1.1
Weak solution for the
\lambda
-problem via Galerkin’s approximation

First of all, for each fixed
\lambda
, we will solve the
\lambda
-problem by the Galerkin approximation and obtain a weak solution. By introducing a basis
(e_{k})_{k=1}^{\infty}
of
L^{2}(\Omega)\cap H^{1}(\Omega)
, and considering the approximation

\displaystyle\xi_{m}(t,y)=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}Z_{j}(t)e_{j}(y),\quad m\geq 2,

(7.5)

\displaystyle w_{m}(t,y)=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\xi_{m}(t,y)=\sum_{k=1}^{m}Z_{j}^{\prime}(t)e_{j}(y),

(7.6)

one can form a system of ODE by multiplying a test vector field
\phi\in\text{span}(e_{1},\cdots,e_{m})
to the
\lambda
-problem. Specifically, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(w_{m}^{\alpha})_{t}\phi_{\alpha}-\int_{\Omega}[(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{m}^{\alpha}]\phi_{\alpha}+\int_{\Omega}[\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}q_{m}]\phi_{\alpha}=0.

(7.7)

We recall that
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)|_{\Gamma}
is tangential to
\Gamma
. Owing to this and the boundary condition of
q_{m}
, i.e.,

\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{3\alpha}q_{m}=-\sigma\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\left(\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}_{m}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}\right)\mathring{\tilde{n}}^{\alpha}+\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(w_{m}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\mathring{\tilde{n}}^{\alpha},\quad\text{on}\,\,\,\Gamma,

(7.8)

where
\mathring{\eta}_{m}
denotes the projection of
\mathring{\eta}
onto
\text{span}(e_{1},\cdots,e_{m})
,
we obtain, after integration by parts, that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(w_{m}^{\alpha})_{t}\phi_{\alpha}+\int_{\Omega}[(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{m}^{\alpha}][(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\phi_{\alpha}]+\kappa\sum_{l=0,1}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(w_{m}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(\phi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}q_{m}[\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\phi_{\alpha}]=\sigma\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}_{m}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\phi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}).

(7.9)

Also, invoking the identity holds for the penalized pressure

\displaystyle\text{div}\,_{\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}w_{m}=-\lambda q_{m},

(7.10)

we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}(w_{m}^{\alpha})_{t}\phi_{\alpha}+\int_{\Omega}[(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{m}^{\alpha}][(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\phi_{\alpha}]+\kappa\sum_{l=0,1}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(w_{m}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(\phi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{\Omega}(\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{m})(\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}\phi)=\sigma\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}_{m}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\phi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}).

(7.11)

Let
\phi=e_{k}
, for each fixed
k=1,2,\cdots,m
. Then (
7.1.1
) yields a system of ODE:

\displaystyle Z_{k}^{\prime\prime}(t)+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\int_{\Omega}[(b_{0}\cdot\partial)e_{j}^{\alpha}][(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(e_{k})_{\alpha}]\right)Z_{j}(t)+\kappa\sum_{l=0,1}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(e_{j}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(e_{k}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\right)Z_{j}^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\lambda}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\int_{\Omega}(\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}e_{j})(\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}e_{k})\right)Z_{j}^{\prime}(t)=\sigma\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}_{m}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(e_{k}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}),

(7.12)

equipped with initial data

\displaystyle Z_{k}(0)=\langle Id,e_{k}\rangle,\quad Z_{k}^{\prime}(0)=\langle v_{0},e_{k}\rangle.

(7.13)

The standard ODE theory gives the the existence and uniqueness of
\xi_{m}
and
w_{m}
in
[0,T_{\lambda}]
for some
T_{\lambda}>0
.
We remark here that it is important to introduce the penalized pressure (
7.10
) because we are not able to formulate a system of ODE directly from (
7.9
). This is because
\phi
is not divergence-free in general.

Setting
\phi=w_{m}
in (
7.1.1
), and since
\sigma|\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}_{m}\cdot\tilde{n}|_{0}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}
, where
\mathcal{N}_{0}
denotes a generic polynomial function such that

\mathcal{N}_{0}=P(\|v_{0}\|_{4.5},\|b_{0}\|_{4.5}),

then

\displaystyle\|w_{m}\|_{0}^{2}+\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{m}\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\|\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{m}\|_{0}^{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}|w_{m}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{1}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.14)

Because
\mathcal{N}_{0}
is independent of
m
,
there is a subsequence, which is still indexed by
m
, satisfying

\displaystyle w_{m}\rightharpoonup w_{\lambda},\quad(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{m}\rightharpoonup(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{\lambda},\quad

\displaystyle\text{in}\,\,L^{\infty}(0,T_{\lambda};L^{2}(\Omega)),

(7.15)

\displaystyle\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{m}\rightharpoonup\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{\lambda},\quad

\displaystyle\text{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T_{\lambda};L^{2}(\Omega)),

(7.16)

\displaystyle w_{m}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}\rightharpoonup w_{\lambda}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}},\quad

\displaystyle\text{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T_{\lambda};H^{1}(\Gamma)),

(7.17)

as
m\to\infty
,
and the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\|w_{\lambda}\|_{0}^{2}+\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{\lambda}\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\|\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{\lambda}\|_{0}^{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}|w_{\lambda}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{1}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.18)

In addition, since
q_{m}=-\frac{1}{\lambda}\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{m}
and
q_{\lambda}=-\frac{1}{\lambda}\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{\lambda}
, (
7.16
) implies that

q_{m}\rightharpoonup q_{\lambda},\quad\text{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T_{\lambda};L^{2}(\Omega)).

(7.19)

Let
Y
be a Banach space, and we denote its dual by
Y^{\prime}
. For
\Psi\in H^{s}(\Omega)^{\prime}
and
\Phi\in H^{s}(\Omega)
, the pairing between
\Psi
and
\Phi
is denoted by
\langle\Psi,\Phi\rangle_{s}
. It follows from the ODE defining
w_{m}
, that
\partial_{t}w_{\lambda}\in L^{2}(0,T_{\lambda};H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime})
, where
H^{s+}:=H^{s+\delta}
for some
0<\delta\ll 1
.
Let
\varphi\in L^{2}(0,T_{\lambda};H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\Omega))
with
\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\|\varphi\|_{\frac{3}{2}+}^{2}=1
be a test vector field. Then there holds the identity:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\langle\partial_{t}w_{\lambda},\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}-\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\langle q_{\lambda},\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}\varphi\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}+}

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\langle(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{\lambda},\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}

(7.20)

\displaystyle+\sigma\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})-\kappa\sum_{l=0,1}\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(w_{\lambda}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}).

Thanks to (
7.18
), we have

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\langle(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{\lambda},\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}=\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\langle(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{\lambda},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\varphi\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}+}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.21)

Also, invoking
\sigma|\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\tilde{n}|_{0}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}
and
\kappa\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}|w_{\lambda}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{1}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}
(inferred from (
7.18
)), we obtain

\text{The second line of the RHS of \eqref{variational eq}}\leq\sigma\mathcal{N}_{0}\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\|\varphi\|_{\frac{1}{2}+}^{2}+\kappa\mathcal{N}_{0}\int_{0}^{T_{\lambda}}\|\varphi\|_{\frac{3}{2}+}^{2}\leq(\sigma+\kappa)\mathcal{N}_{0}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.22)

and so

\text{RHS of \eqref{variational eq}}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.23)

Since
\partial_{t}w_{\lambda}\in L^{2}(0,T_{\lambda};H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime})
, (
7.20
) and (
7.23
) indicate

q_{\lambda}\in L^{2}(0,T_{\lambda};H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime}),\quad\text{and}\,\,\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}q_{\lambda}\in L^{2}(0,T_{\lambda};H^{-1}(\Omega)).

(7.24)

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w_{\lambda}-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{\lambda}+\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}q_{\lambda}=0,\quad\text{in}\,\,\,L^{2}(0,T_{\lambda};H^{-1}(\Omega)).

(7.25)

7.1.2
The limit as
\lambda\rightarrow 0

Because
\mathcal{N}_{0}
is independent of
\lambda
, the energy estimate (
7.18
) indicates that

\|w_{\lambda}\|_{0}^{2}+\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{\lambda}\|_{0}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

Thus, there exists a
\lambda
-independent

T>0
, such that the sequences
\{w_{\lambda}\}
and
\{(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{\lambda}\}
admit converging subsequences (still indexed by
\lambda
) as
\lambda\to 0
, i.e.,

w_{\lambda}\rightharpoonup v,\quad(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\xi_{\lambda}\rightharpoonup(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta,\quad\text{in}\,\,L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)).

(7.26)

Furthermore, we must have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lambda}\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{\lambda}\|_{0}^{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}|w_{\lambda}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{1}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0},

and thus

\displaystyle\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{\lambda}\rightharpoonup\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}v=0,\quad

\displaystyle\text{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega)),

(7.27)

\displaystyle w_{\lambda}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}\rightharpoonup v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}\quad

\displaystyle\text{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma)).

(7.28)

Moreover, the following energy estimate holds:

\displaystyle\|v\|_{0}^{2}+\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{1}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.29)

Our next goal is to show that
(\eta,v)
is indeed a weak solution for (
7.2
). To achieve this, we need to show
\partial_{t}w_{\lambda}
converges to
\partial_{t}v
, and
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{\lambda}
converges to
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta
in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime})
. In addition, we need to find
q
, in terms of the pressure function, that belongs to
L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime})
.
First, analogous to (
7.20
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\langle\partial_{t}w_{\lambda},\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}+\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{\lambda}\langle\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{\lambda},\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}\varphi\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}+}

(7.30)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{T}\langle(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{\lambda},\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}

\displaystyle+\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})-\kappa\sum_{l=0,1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(w_{\lambda}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}).

Second, let

q:=\lim_{\lambda\to 0}q_{\lambda}=-\lim_{\lambda\to 0}\frac{1}{\lambda}\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{\lambda},

(7.31)

where the limit

-\lim_{\lambda\to 0}\frac{1}{\lambda}\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{\lambda}

exists in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime})
owing to
\partial_{t}w_{\lambda}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime})
, and the RHS of (
7.30
)
\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}
, which is independent of
\lambda
. We then have
q\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime})
and
\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}q\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{-1}(\Omega))
, analogous to (
7.24
). Invoking (
7.26
), this yields

\partial_{t}w_{\lambda}\rightharpoonup\partial_{t}v,\quad\text{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime}),

(7.32)

as well as

\lim_{\lambda\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}w_{\lambda}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime}}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.33)

On the other hand, by employing similar arguments on

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\langle(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{\lambda},\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}=-\int_{0}^{T}\langle\partial_{t}w_{\lambda},\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{\lambda}\langle\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}w_{\lambda},\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}\varphi\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}+}

(7.34)

\displaystyle+\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})-\kappa\sum_{l=0,1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(w_{\lambda}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}),

we have

(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{\lambda}\rightharpoonup(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta,\quad\text{in}\,\,L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime}),

(7.35)

and

\lim_{\lambda\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{\lambda}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime}}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.36)

Lastly, we infer from (
7.30
), and the convergence of
\partial_{t}w_{\lambda}
,
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\xi_{\lambda}
, and
q_{\lambda}
, that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\langle\partial_{t}v,\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}+\int_{0}^{T}\langle(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta,\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}-\int_{0}^{T}\langle q,\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}\varphi\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}+}

(7.37)

\displaystyle=\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})-\kappa\sum_{l=0,1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}).

This implies that
(\eta,v,q)
verifies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta+\nabla_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}q=0,\quad\text{and}\,\,\,\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}v=0,\quad\text{in}\,\,\,L^{2}(0,T;H^{-1}(\Omega)),

(7.38)

and so we’ve shown that
\eta,v
is indeed a weak solution for (
7.2
).

Remark
.

We find that it is easier to construct
q
directly by (
7.31
) instead of employing the representation argument used in Section 8 of
[
11
]
.

Finally, we consider the difference between (
7.37
) with
v
and
v^{\prime}
, respectively, i.e.,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{T}\langle\partial_{t}(v-v^{\prime}),\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}+\int_{0}^{T}\langle(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}(\eta-\eta^{\prime}),\varphi\rangle_{\frac{3}{2}+}+\kappa\sum_{l=0,1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}((v-v^{\prime})\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\\
-\int_{0}^{T}\langle(q-q^{\prime}),\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}\varphi\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}+}=0.\end{aligned}

(7.39)

where
(\eta^{\prime},v^{\prime},q^{\prime})
is assumed to be another solution with the initial data. The uniqueness of the weak solution follows from setting
\varphi=v-v^{\prime}
.

7.2
H^{1}
-Regularity estimates of
v
,
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
and
q

We shall show that
v
,
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
and
q
are in fact
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))
.
Let

e(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\|\eta\|_{1}^{2}+\|v\|_{1}^{2}+\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1}^{2}\,dt,\quad t\in[0,T].

(7.40)

Our goal is to show

e(T)\leq\frac{1}{\kappa}\mathcal{N}_{0},

(7.41)

for some
T=T(\mathcal{N}_{0},\kappa)
.
It suffices to consider
\int_{0}^{T}\|v\|_{1}^{2}
and
\int_{0}^{T}\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1}^{2}
only
since

\int_{0}^{T}\|\eta\|_{1}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\|\eta_{0}\|_{1}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|v\|_{1}^{2}\,dt\Big)\,dt.

Thanks to Lemma
2.5
(2), it suffices for us to control

\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{div}\,v\|_{0}^{2},\quad\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{curl }v\|_{0}^{2},\quad\int_{0}^{T}|v^{3}|_{0.5}^{2},

as well as

\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{div}\,(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2},\quad\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{curl }(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2},\quad\int_{0}^{T}|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{0.5}^{2},

in order to control
\int_{0}^{T}\|v\|_{1}^{2}
and
\int_{0}^{T}\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1}^{2}
.

7.2.1
Control of the divergence and curl

The estimates we need here are essentially the same as those in Section
3.2
but without considering the time differentiated quantities. First, since (
3.18
) in Lemma
3.3
remains true with
\tilde{A}
replaced by
\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{div}\,v\|_{0}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|(\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}-\delta^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\|_{0}^{2}\leq\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial v\|_{0}^{2}\leq\varepsilon e(T).

(7.42)

Second, because
\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
verifies the evolution equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)=[\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]v+(\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}).

(7.43)

So, one needs to bound

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\text{RHS of}\,\,\eqref{div b eq}\|_{0}^{2}\,dt

in order to control
\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\|_{0}^{2}
.
We have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\|_{0}^{2}\,dt\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\|_{0}^{2}\,dt

(7.44)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{0}\|\partial((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\|_{0}^{2}\,dt\leq T\mathcal{N}_{0}e(T).

(7.45)

Moreover, by writing
[\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]v=\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}((\partial_{\mu}b_{0})\cdot\partial)v_{\alpha}-((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha})\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}
, one gets

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|[\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]v\|_{0}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{0}\|\partial v\|_{0}^{2}\,dt\leq T\mathcal{N}_{0}e(T).

(7.46)

Thus,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)\|_{0}^{2}\leq T\mathcal{N}_{0}e(T).

(7.47)

In addition, since

\displaystyle\|\text{div}\,(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}\leq\|\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}+\|\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}-\delta\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2},

invoking (
3.18
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{div}\,(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}\leq\varepsilon e(T)+T\mathcal{N}_{0}e(T).

(7.48)

Third, the evolution equation satisfied by
\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}v
and
\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
reads

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}v)_{\alpha}-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)_{\alpha}=[\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)]((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta)_{\alpha}+\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}v_{\alpha},

(7.49)

and this yields the following
L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
energy identity after testing with
\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}v
and integrating in space and time:

\displaystyle\|\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\|[(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}](b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}v\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|[(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}](b_{0}\cdot\partial)v\|_{0}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}.

Integrating in time one more time, we achieve

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\|\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}\Big)\lesssim

\displaystyle T\mathcal{N}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\|[(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}](b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}v\|_{0}^{2}\Big)\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\|[(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}](b_{0}\cdot\partial)v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}\Big)\,dt.

It suffices to control the first two terms on the RHS since the third and fourth terms can then be controlled by an analogous method with the same bound.

For the first term on the RHS, since one can express

[(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}](b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}((b_{0}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\nu\beta})\partial_{\nu}\eta^{\gamma}-\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\nu\beta}(\partial_{\nu}b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{\gamma}

and so

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|[(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}](b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta_{\alpha}\|_{0}\,dt\lesssim T\mathcal{N}_{0}e(T).

(7.50)

Similarly,

\|[(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}](b_{0}\cdot\partial)v_{\alpha}\|_{0}\leq T\mathcal{N}_{0}e(T).

(7.51)

In addition, for the second term, writing
\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\mathring{A}}v=\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\partial_{t}\mathring{A}^{\nu\beta})\partial_{\nu}v^{\gamma}
, one obtains

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\|\text{curl }_{\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}}v\|_{0}\,dt\leq T\mathcal{N}_{0}e(T).

(7.52)

Summing these up, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\|\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}v\|_{0}^{2}+\|\text{curl}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}\Big)\,dt\leq T\mathcal{N}_{0}e(T).

(7.53)

7.2.2
Control of the boundary terms

First we state some supplementary results which will come in handy when treating the boundary estimates. The following inequality is a direct consequence of (
2.10
). Let
f\in H^{0.5}(\partial\Omega)
and
g
be a smooth function. Then

\displaystyle|fg|_{0.5}\lesssim|f|_{0.5}|g|_{1+}.

(7.54)

Also, we remark here that (
3.20
), (
3.21
) remain true by replacing
\tilde{n}
by
\mathring{\tilde{n}}
.

Control of
\int_{0}^{T}|v^{3}|_{0.5}^{2}
:

It suffices to control
\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{0.5}^{2}
since

\int_{0}^{T}|v^{3}|_{0.5}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{0.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot(\mathring{\tilde{n}}-N)|_{0.5}^{2},

(7.55)

where, after invoking (
7.54
) and the trace lemma, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot(\mathring{\tilde{n}}-N)|_{0.5}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}|v|_{0.5}^{2}|\mathring{\tilde{n}}-N|_{1+}^{2}\lesssim\varepsilon e(T).

(7.56)

Moreover, the control of
\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{0.5}^{2}
is a direct consequence of (
7.29
),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{0.5}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{\kappa}\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{1}^{2}\leq\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}}{\kappa}.

(7.57)

Control of
\int_{0}^{T}|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{0.5}^{2}
:

Similar to the control
\int_{0}^{T}|v^{3}|_{0.5}^{2}
, it suffices to bound
\int_{0}^{T}|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{0.5}^{2}
only.

Since
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)|_{\Gamma}=b_{0}\cdot\overline{\partial}{}
and
\overline{\partial}{}(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{t=0}=\overline{\partial}{}\eta^{3}|_{t=0}=0
, we have

(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})=\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\,dt.

(7.58)

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{0.5}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\right|^{2}_{0.5}\,dt\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}|\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt,

by Jensen’s inequality. Here, the term on the RHS is equal to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{0.5}^{2}\,dt+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{0.5}^{2}=I+II.

Since
\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{n}}=Q(\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}})\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{v}}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}
, invoking (
7.54
) and the trace lemma, we have
II\leq T\mathcal{N}_{0}e(T)
.
Next, invoking (
7.54
), we have

\displaystyle I\lesssim||b_{0}||_{0.5}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{2}^{2}\,dt.

By employing the boundary condition we obtain the following elliptic equation verified by
v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}
on
\Gamma
:

\displaystyle\overline{\Delta}{}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})=\frac{1}{\kappa}\Big((v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})+\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q+\sigma\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}\Big).

(7.59)

By the virtual of the elliptic estimate, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{2+}^{2}\,dt\leq\frac{C}{\kappa^{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{0+}^{2}+|\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q|_{0+}^{2}+\sigma|\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}^{3}|_{0+}^{2}\Big)\,dt,

(7.60)

where
C
denotes a generic constant.
It is clear that the third term can be controlled by
\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}T\mathcal{N}_{0}
, and the first term is bounded by
\frac{1}{\kappa^{3}}T\mathcal{N}_{0}
in view of (
7.57
). Therefore,

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{2+}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}T\mathcal{N}_{0}+\frac{1}{\kappa^{3}}T\mathcal{N}_{0}+\frac{1}{\kappa}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{N}_{0}|q|_{0+}^{2}\,dt.

(7.61)

Now, we impose the bootstrap assumption

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|q\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega)}^{2}\leq\frac{\mathcal{N}_{0}}{\kappa}.

(7.62)

In light of this and the trace lemma, the second term on the RHS of (
7.61
) is bounded by
\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}T\mathcal{N}_{0}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{2+}^{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}+\frac{1}{\kappa^{3}}\right)T\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.63)

In summary, we have

e(T)\leq\frac{1}{\kappa}\mathcal{N}_{0}+\left(\frac{1}{\kappa^{2}}+\frac{1}{\kappa^{3}}\right)T\mathcal{N}_{0}+\varepsilon e(T)+T\mathcal{N}_{0}e(T),

(7.64)

and this implies (
7.41
) if
T
is chosen sufficiently small, say
T=\min(\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{N}_{0}},\kappa^{3})
.

Closing the bootstrap argument:
We test (
7.2
) with
\varphi\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega))
,
\|\varphi\|_{\frac{1}{2}+}=1
, and integrate in time, to get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\langle\partial_{t}v,\varphi\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}+}+\int_{0}^{T}\langle(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta,\varphi\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}+}-\int_{0}^{T}\langle q,\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}\varphi\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}+}

(7.65)

\displaystyle=\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})-\kappa\sum_{l=0,1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}).

The first term on the RHS is
\leq\sigma\mathcal{N}_{0}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}
. For the second term on the RHS,
we integrate
\overline{\partial}{}
by parts and obtain

\displaystyle-\kappa\sum_{l=0,1}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})=\kappa\sum_{l=0,1,2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\overline{\partial}{}^{l}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\varphi\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\mathcal{N}_{0}\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{2}

It suffices to control
\kappa\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{2+}
. Invoking (
7.59
) and we have, by the elliptic estimate, that

\kappa\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{2+}\leq\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{0+}+\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q|_{0+}+\int_{0}^{T}\sigma|\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}^{3}|_{0+}.

(7.66)

The third term is bounded by
T\mathcal{N}_{0}
. We square the second term and then apply Jensen’s inequality to obtain

\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q|_{0+}\right)^{2}\leq T\mathcal{N}_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\|q\|_{\frac{1}{2}+}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}^{2},

(7.67)

where the last inequality follows from
T\leq\kappa^{3}\leq\kappa
, and so

\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q|_{0+}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.68)

Similarly,

\left(\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{0+}\right)^{2}\leq T\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{0.5+}^{2}\leq\frac{T}{\kappa}\mathcal{N}_{0},

(7.69)

after invoking (
7.57
), and so

\int_{0}^{T}|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{0+}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.70)

Summing up, the RHS of (
7.65
) is bounded by
\mathcal{N}_{0}
.
Therefore, by employing arguments parallel to (
7.30
)-(
7.36
), we have

\lim_{\lambda\to 0}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}v\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime}}^{2}\right)\leq\mathcal{N}_{0}.

(7.71)

Moreover, by employing the Lagrange multiplier lemma (i.e.,
[
11
, Lemma 7.4]
),
(
7.65
) implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|q\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega)}^{2}\leq\mathcal{N}_{0},

(7.72)

which improves the bootstrap assumption (
7.62
) whenever
\kappa
is fixed to be sufficiently small.

Remark
.

We need to modify the Lagrange multiplier lemma slightly. Since we need our
q\in H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega)
, we need to consider the linear functional

\langle\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}\varphi,q\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}+}

defined on
X(t)
,
where
X(t)=\{\varphi:\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}\varphi\in H^{\frac{1}{2}+}(\Omega)^{\prime}\}
.

7.2.3
The strong solution for the linearized equations

Since
v,(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))
and so
\partial_{t}v,(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
, we can now adapt Lemma
2.6
to bound
q
in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))
.

Therefore, we have obtained a strong solution for the linearized
\kappa
-problem (
7.2
). This allows us to further boost the regularity of the linearized solution to
H^{4.5}(\Omega)
via classical methods in the upcoming section. Then we can achieve a solution for the nonlinear
\kappa
-problem by approximating it by a sequence of linearized solutions.

8
Existence for the nonlinear approximate
\kappa
-problem

We aim to construct a solution for the nonlinear
\kappa
-problem for fixed
0<\kappa\ll 1
. Let
(\eta_{(0)},v_{(0)})=(Id,0)
. For each
m\geq 0
, Let
(\eta_{(m)},v_{(m)},q_{(m)})
be the solution for

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\eta_{(m)}=v_{(m)}~~~&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
\partial_{t}v_{(m)}-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta_{(m)}+\nabla_{\tilde{A}_{(m-1)}}q_{(m)}=0~~~&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
\text{div}\,_{\tilde{A}_{(m-1)}}v_{(m)}=0,\quad\text{div }b_{0}=0&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
v_{(m)}^{3}=b_{0}^{3}=0~~~&\text{on}~\Gamma_{0};\\
\tilde{A}_{(m-1)}^{3\alpha}q_{(m)}=-\sigma\sqrt{g_{(m-1)}}(\Delta_{g_{(m-1)}}\eta_{(m-1)}\cdot\tilde{n}_{(m-1)})\tilde{n}_{(m-1)}^{\alpha}+\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v_{(m)}\cdot\tilde{n}_{(m-1)})\tilde{n}_{(m-1)}^{\alpha}~~~&\text{on}~\Gamma;\\
(\eta_{(m)},v_{(m)})=(Id,v_{0})~~~&\text{on}~\{t=0\}{\times}\overline{\Omega}.\end{cases}

(8.1)

Here, the (linearized) coefficients, e.g.,
\tilde{A}_{(m-1)},g_{(m-1)},\tilde{n}_{(m-1)}
are determined by
(\eta_{(m-1)},v_{(m-1)})
.

The goal is to prove that the sequence
\{(\eta_{{(m)}},v_{{(m)}})\}_{m\geq 0}
strongly converges (and so does
q_{(m)}
), and the limit verifies the nonlinear approximate
\kappa
-problem. This is done by applying Picard’s iteration.

8.1
A priori estimate of the linearized approximate problem

We first establish the
H^{4.5}
-energy estimate for
(\eta_{{(m)}},v_{{(m)}})
, and then this estimate can be carried over to the difference between two successive systems (
8.1
) which yields the convergence of
(\eta_{{(m)}},v_{{(m)}})
in
H^{3.5}
as
m\to\infty
. Let
m\geq 1
be fixed and assume the solutions
(\eta_{(l)},v_{(l)})
are known for all
l\leq m
. For the sake of clean notations, we will denote
(\eta_{(m)},v_{(m)})
by
(\eta,v)
and
(\eta_{(m-1)},v_{(m-1)})
by
(\mathring{\eta},\mathring{v})
if no confusion is raised. Let
\tilde{A}_{(m-1)}:=\mathring{\tilde{{A}}},g_{(m-1)}:=\mathring{g},\tilde{n}_{(m-1)}:=\mathring{\tilde{n}}
. Then (
8.1
) reduces exactly to the linearized system (
7.2
).

Proposition 8.1
.

For each fixed
\kappa>0
,
there exists some
T_{\kappa}>0
such that the solution
(\eta,v)
for (
7.2
) satisfies

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathcal{E}(t)\leq\mathcal{C},

(8.2)

where
\mathcal{C}
is a constant depends on
\|v_{0}\|_{4.5}
,
\|b_{0}\|_{4.5},|v_{0}|_{5}
, provided that

\displaystyle\|\mathring{\tilde{{J}}}(t)-1\|_{3.5}+\|Id-\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}(t)\|_{3.5}+\|Id-\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{T}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}(t)\|_{3.5}\leq\varepsilon.

(8.3)

holds for all
t\in[0,T_{\kappa}]
.
Here the energy functional
\mathcal{E}
of (
7.2
) is defined to be

\mathcal{E}(t)=\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(t)+\mathcal{E}^{(2)}(t),

(8.4)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(1)}(t)
