\displaystyle:=\left\|\eta\right\|_{4.5}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{4.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}v\right\|_{3.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\right\|_{2.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{1.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{4.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{3.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{2.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{1.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(2)}(t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}\right|_{1}^{2}\,dt+\kappa\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1.5}^{2}\bigg).

Thanks to the Gronwall’s inequality, (
8.2
) is a direct consequence of

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathcal{E}(t)\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{C}(\|v_{0}\|_{4.5},\|b_{0}\|_{4.5},|v_{0}|_{5})+C(\varepsilon)\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathcal{E}(t)+(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}\mathcal{P})\int_{0}^{T_{\kappa}}\mathcal{P},

(8.5)

where
\mathcal{P}=P(\mathcal{E}(t),\|\mathring{v}\|_{4.5},\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\eta}\|_{4.5})
(after a slight abuse of notations). Also, since
\kappa
is fixed, we will drop the subscript
\kappa
and denote
T_{\kappa}=T
for the sake of clean notations. Similar to (
3.13
) we shall assume that
\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathcal{E}(t)=\mathcal{E}(T)
, and this allows us to drop
\sup\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}}
in (
8.5
). In other words, we only need to show

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(T)\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)\mathcal{E}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(8.6)

where
\mathcal{P}_{0}=\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{E}(0),\|q(0)\|_{4.5},\|q_{t}(0)\|_{3.5},\|q_{tt}(0)\|_{2.5})
. Also, it suffices to put
\mathcal{P}_{0}
on the RHS of (
8.6
) since (
6.2
) allows us to control
\mathcal{P}_{0}
by
\mathcal{C}
.

Remark
.

It can be seen that
\mathcal{E}(t)
constructed above is significantly simpler than
E_{\kappa}(t)
given in (
3.9
). The main reason is that the energy estimate for (
8.4
) does not have to be uniform in
\kappa
. Instead, the right side of (
8.6
) may depend on
\kappa^{-1}
. Besides, the surface tension term
-\sigma\sqrt{\mathring{g}}(\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\mathring{\tilde{n}}^{\alpha}
now serves as a given source term in the boundary condition. Based on these two facts, we can significantly simplify the following steps, compared with the nonlinear estimates in Section
4
– Section
6
.

1.

Elliptic estimates for the boundary normal traces.
Unlike section
3.3
, we now regard the boundary condition of (
7.2
) as an elliptic equation of
v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}

\overline{\Delta}{}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})=v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}-\kappa^{-1}\left(\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q+\sigma\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}\right).

Since the surface tension term is a given fixed-term here, one can directly control
v\cdot\hat{n}
(and its time derivatives) via elliptic estimates. Therefore, we now only need to perform the tangential energy estimate of full-time derivatives. We refer to Subsection
8.1.1
for the details.

2.

Removing extra (tangential) spatial derivatives via the mollifier properties.
In light of (
2.20
), we can absorb additional tangential spatial derivatives when necessary. This will allow us to greatly simplify most of the estimates including the surface tension terms, artificial viscosity terms, and the error terms involving
N-\mathring{\tilde{n}}
on the boundary. Compared to
E_{\kappa}^{(2)},E_{\kappa}^{(3)}
in (
3.9
), much fewer
\kappa
-weighed higher-order terms are needed here in
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}(t)
to close the energy estimates. In addition to that, we do not need to establish the improved boundary regularity as in Lemma
3.5
.

We will mainly focus on the simplified parts as mentioned above in the following calculations. The div-curl estimates and the elliptic estimates for
q
are essentially identical to Section
3.1
, so we will only outline them in Subsection
8.1.3
but without repeating the detailed proof.

8.1.1
Boundary estimates

This subsection is devoted to the control of boundary normal traces
|\partial_{t}^{k}v^{3}|_{4-k}
and
|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{4-k}
for
k=0,1,2,3
.

Lemma 8.2
.

For
k=0,1,2,3
, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{k}v^{3}|_{4-k}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)\mathcal{E}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(8.7)

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{4-k}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)\mathcal{E}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(8.8)

Note that we no longer require the energy bound to be
\kappa
-independent. Hence,
we are able to use (
2.20
) to absorb extra tangential spatial derivatives on the smoothed variables, i.e., variables with
\sim
on top.
We can absorb at most two tangential spatial derivatives since
\tilde{\cdot}=\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}\cdot
on the boundary. Recall that the boundary condition in the linearized equations reads

\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q=-\sigma\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}+\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}).

(8.9)

This can be converted to an elliptic equation satisfied by
v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}
, i.e.,

\overline{\Delta}{}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})=v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}-\kappa^{-1}\left(\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q+\sigma\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}\right).

(8.10)

Now, invoking the standard elliptic estimate and (
2.6
), we get

\displaystyle|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{4}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{2}^{2}+\kappa^{-1}\left(\left|\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q\right|_{2}^{2}+\sigma P(|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}},|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\mathring{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}})|\mathring{\eta}|_{4}^{2}\right)

(8.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

where the used the trace lemma and (
3.36
) in the second inequality.

For the magnetic field, since
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)=b_{0}^{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}
on
\Gamma
and hence
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{t=0}=0
. Thus,

(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})=\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{t}\Big((b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\Big)=\int_{0}^{T}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})+\int_{0}^{T}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{n}}).

(8.12)

Since
\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{n}}=-\mathring{\tilde{g}}^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\mathring{\tilde{v}}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}=Q(\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}})\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{v}}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}
, and invoking (
2.20
) and the Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{n}})\right|_{4}^{2}\lesssim T\int_{0}^{T}|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{4}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}

(8.13)

Here, we need (
2.20
) in order to control the leading order term generated when
\overline{\partial}{}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
fall on
\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{v}}
(which is part of
\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{n}}
), i.e.,

\int_{0}^{T}Q(|\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}|_{L^{\infty}},|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}|_{L^{\infty}})|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{v}}|_{4}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{T}|b_{0}|_{4}^{2}Q(|\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}|_{L^{\infty}},|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\mathring{\tilde{v}}|_{4}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}|b_{0}|_{4}^{2}Q(|\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}|_{L^{\infty}},|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}|_{L^{\infty}})|\mathring{v}|_{4}^{2}.

In addition,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{T}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\right|_{4}^{2}\lesssim T\int_{0}^{T}|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{4}^{2},

(8.14)

and the RHS can be controlled by studying the elliptic equation satisfied by
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})
. Taking
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
on (
8.10
) and we get

\overline{\Delta}{}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})=[\overline{\Delta}{},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)](v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})+(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})-\kappa^{-1}\left((b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q)+\sigma(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\right),

(8.15)

then the elliptic estimate implies

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}P(|b_{0}|_{4})\int_{0}^{T}\left(|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{4}^{2}+\left|\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q\right|_{3}^{2}+\sigma P(|\mathring{\eta}|_{H^{4}})|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\mathring{\eta}|_{4}^{2}\right)\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(8.16)

Thus,

|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{4}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(8.17)

We can obtain the bounds for
|v^{3}|_{4}^{2}
and
|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{4}^{2}
from (
8.11
) and (
8.17
), respectively. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle|v^{3}|_{4}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle|v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{4}^{2}+|v\cdot(N-\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{4}^{2},

(8.18)

\displaystyle|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{4}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{4}^{2}+|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot(N-\mathring{\tilde{n}}))|_{4}^{2}.

(8.19)

Since

N-\mathring{\tilde{n}}=-\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{n}}=\int_{0}^{T}Q(\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}})\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{v}}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}},

invoking (
2.20
) and the proof of (
3.21
) , we have

\displaystyle|N-\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{5}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(8.20)

Therefore,

\displaystyle|v\cdot(N-\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{4}^{2}+|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)(\eta\cdot(N-\mathring{\tilde{n}}))|_{4}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(8.21)

Now, we can take time derivative
\partial_{t}
in (
8.10
) to get the elliptic equation of
\partial_{t}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})
on the boundary, i.e.,

\displaystyle\overline{\Delta}{}\partial_{t}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})-\kappa^{-1}\left(\partial_{t}(\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}q)+\sigma\partial_{t}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\right).

(8.22)

Then standard elliptic estimate gives

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{3}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{1}^{2}+\left|\partial_{t}\left(\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{33}q\right)\right|_{1}^{2}+\sigma P(|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}},|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\mathring{\eta}|_{L^{\infty}},|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{v}|_{L^{\infty}})|\mathring{v}|_{3}^{2}

(8.23)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

This estimate implies the estimate for
|\partial_{t}v^{3}|_{3}^{2}
by writing
|\partial_{t}v^{3}|_{3}^{2}\leq|\partial_{t}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{3}^{2}+|\partial_{t}(v\cdot(N-\mathring{\tilde{n}}))|_{3}^{2}
and then adapting the arguments from (
8.18
)-(
8.21
).
Moreover, in light of the estimate for
|v^{3}|_{4}^{2}
, we have

|\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{3}=|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)v^{3}|_{3}^{2}\leq P(|b_{0}|_{3})|v^{3}|_{4}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(8.24)

Similarly, by taking two time derivatives to (
8.10
), we can control
|\partial_{t}^{2}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{2}
by the standard elliptic estimate, i.e.,

|\partial_{t}^{2}(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{2}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(8.25)

and this yields

|\partial_{t}^{2}v^{3}|_{2}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(8.26)

In addition to this,
|\partial_{t}^{2}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{2}^{2}
reduces to
|\partial_{t}v^{3}|_{3}^{2}
, whose bound is given above. Also, in the case when there are three time derivatives,
|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{1}^{2}
reduces to
|\partial_{t}^{2}v^{3}|_{2}^{2}
, which is just (
8.26
).

Finally,
|\partial_{t}^{3}v^{3}|_{1}^{2}
can be controlled with the help
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}
. We can make use of the
\kappa
-weighted higher-order terms to directly control the time-integrated terms on the boundary. Specifically, by writing

|\partial_{t}^{3}v^{3}|_{1}\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{t}^{4}v^{3}|_{1},

we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{t}^{3}v^{3}|_{1}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{t}^{4}v^{3}|_{1}\right)^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{t}^{4}v^{3}|_{1}^{2},

(8.27)

where

\displaystyle T\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{t}^{4}v^{3}|_{1}^{2}\leq T\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{1}^{2}+T\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot(N-\mathring{\tilde{n}})|_{1}^{2}.

(8.28)

Here, the second term on the RHS is
\lesssim\kappa^{-1}TC(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}
whereas the first term is
\lesssim\kappa^{-1}T\int_{0}^{T}|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{1}^{2}
. Therefore, by choosing
T
sufficiently small, we have

|\partial_{t}^{3}v^{3}|_{1}^{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)\mathcal{E}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}.

(8.29)

8.1.2
Tangential estimate with four time derivatives

We still need to control

\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0}^{2},\quad\|\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}

in order to finish the control of
\mathcal{E}
. In fact, we only need to control
\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{0}^{2}
since
\|\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{0}^{2}
reduces to
\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1}^{2}
which has been done previously.

Now we compute the
L^{2}
-estimate of
\partial_{t}^{4}v
and
\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
. Invoking (
7.2
) and integrating
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
by parts, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}^{4}v|^{2}+\left|\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right|^{2}\,dy\,dt=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}(\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}q)\,dy\,dt

(8.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}q\,dy\,dt\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}~\left[\partial_{t}^{4},\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}\right]\partial_{\mu}q\,dy\,dt}_{\mathring{I}_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q\,dy\,dt\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q\,dS\,dt}_{\mathring{I}_{B}}\underbrace{+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{0}}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q\,dS\,dt}_{=0}+\mathring{I}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\underbrace{\partial_{t}^{4}(\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}v)}_{=0}\partial_{t}^{4}q\,dy\,dt\underbrace{+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\left[\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha},\partial_{t}^{4}\right]\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}~\partial_{t}^{4}q\,dy\,dt}_{\mathring{I}_{2}}+\mathring{I}_{B}+\mathring{I}_{1}.

Here,
\mathring{I}_{1}
and
\mathring{I}_{2}
can be straightforwardly controlled by
\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}
. We start to analyze the boundary integral
I_{B}
.

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{B}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\alpha}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}^{4}q=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}(\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\partial_{t}^{4}q)

(8.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}(\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\partial_{t}^{4}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\tilde{g}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Delta_{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}(\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\partial_{t}^{4}\left(\mathring{\tilde{g}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\right):=\mathring{I}_{B1}+\mathring{I}_{B2}.

Invoking the identity (
2.6
), we have

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{B1}=

\displaystyle\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}(\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\partial_{t}^{4}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\tilde{g}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathring{g}^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})

(8.32)

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}\sqrt{\mathring{\tilde{g}}}(\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\partial_{t}^{4}(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\mathring{\tilde{g}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathring{g}^{ij}\mathring{g}^{kl}\overline{\partial}{}_{l}\mathring{\eta}^{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\mathring{\eta}_{\mu}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\mathring{\eta}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{B11}+\mathring{I}_{B12}.

Since

\mathring{I}_{B11}\overset{L}{=}\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\mathring{g}^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{v}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})

we integrate
\overline{\partial}{}_{i}
by parts and get

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{B11}\overset{L}{=}

\displaystyle-\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(\sqrt{\mathring{g}}\mathring{g}^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{v}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}\leq\varepsilon\mathcal{E}(T)+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

and
\mathring{I}_{B12}
can be treated in the same fashion.

Next we study
\mathring{I}_{B2}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{B2}\overset{L}{=}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\Delta}{}(\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})\overline{\Delta}{}(v\cdot\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\tilde{n}}):=\mathring{I}_{B21}+\mathring{I}_{B22},

(8.33)

where
\mathring{I}_{B21}
contributes to the positive energy term
\int_{0}^{T}|\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}|_{1}^{2}
after integrating
\overline{\partial}{}
by parts and moving the resulting term to the LHS. In addition, since
\partial_{t}^{4}\mathring{\tilde{n}}=Q(\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}})\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{\tilde{v}}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}}+
lower-order terms,

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{B22}\overset{L}{=}

\displaystyle-\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma}(\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})(v\cdot Q(\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}})\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{\tilde{v}}\cdot\mathring{\tilde{n}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\varepsilon\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}|v|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}Q(|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{\tilde{v}}|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}

\displaystyle\varepsilon\mathcal{E}(T)+\int_{0}^{T}|v|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}Q(|\overline{\partial}{}\mathring{\tilde{{\eta}}}|_{L^{\infty}})|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{v}|_{0}^{2}\leq\varepsilon\mathcal{E}(T)+\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P},

(8.34)

where we used (
2.20
) in the second to the last inequality to control
|\overline{\partial}{}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{\tilde{v}}|_{0}^{2}\lesssim\kappa^{-1}|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{3}\mathring{v}|_{0}^{2}
.

8.1.3
Interior estimates

To control
\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4.5-k}^{2}
and
\|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5-k}^{2}
,
k=0,1,2,3,
we only need to apply the div-curl estimate:

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{4.5-k}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}\text{div}\,v\|_{3.5-k}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{k}\text{curl }v\|_{3.5-k}^{2}+|\partial_{t}^{k}v^{3}|_{4-k}^{2},

(8.35)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{4.5-k}^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}\text{div}\,(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5-k}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{k}\text{curl }(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{3.5-k}^{2}+|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{4-k}^{2}.

(8.36)

The boundary terms
|\partial_{t}^{k}v^{3}|_{4-k}^{2}
and
|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta^{3}|_{4-k}^{2}
are treated in Lemma
8.2
.
The estimates for the divergence and curl of
v
and
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta
, together with their time derivatives are identical to those in Section
3.2
, and so we shall not repeat the proofs. Furthermore, the estimate for the top order interior term in
\mathcal{E}^{(2)}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\kappa\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1.5}^{2}\Big)\leq\mathcal{P}_{0}+C(\varepsilon)\mathcal{E}(T)+\mathcal{P}\int_{0}^{T}\mathcal{P}

(8.37)

is identical to what has been done in Section
5
.

We also need the estimates for the interior Sobolev norms of the pressure
q
.
q
satisfies the following elliptic system

\begin{cases}-\Delta_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}q=\partial_{t}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}\partial_{\mu}v_{\alpha}+\text{div}_{\mathring{\tilde{A}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\frac{\partial q}{\partial N}=(\delta^{\mu 3}-\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{\mu\alpha}\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{3\alpha})\partial_{\mu}q-\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}v_{\alpha}+\mathring{\tilde{{A}}}^{3\alpha}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}\eta_{\alpha}&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
\frac{\partial q}{\partial N}=0&\text{ on }\Gamma_{0}.\end{cases}

(8.38)

The elliptic estimates are identical to (
3.36
) in Section
3.1
, so we omit the details here. This concludes the proof of Proposition
8.1
.

8.2
The Picard iteration

We now prove that the sequence
\{(\eta_{{(m)}},v_{(m)},q_{(m)})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
has a strongly convergent subsequence. We define
[f]_{{(m)}}:=f_{{(m+1)}}-f_{{(m)}}
for any function
f
and then
([\eta]_{(m)},[v]_{(m)},[q]_{(m)})
satisfies the following system

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}[\eta]_{(m)}=[v]_{(m)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\partial_{t}[v]_{(m)}-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}[\eta]_{{(m)}}+\nabla_{\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}}[q]_{{(m)}}=-\nabla_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}q_{(m)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
\text{div }_{\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}}[v]_{(m)}=-\text{div }_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}v_{(m)}~~~&\text{ in }\Omega,\\
[q]_{(m)}=\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})([v]_{(m)}\cdot\tilde{n}_{(m)})+h_{{(m)}}~~~&\text{ on }\Gamma,\\
([\eta]_{(m)},[v]_{{(m)}})|_{t=0}=(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0}).\end{cases}

(8.39)

where

\displaystyle h_{(m)}=

\displaystyle\kappa(1-\overline{\Delta}{})(v_{(m)}\cdot[\tilde{n}]_{{(m-1)}})

\displaystyle-\sigma\left(\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{{(m)}}}g_{{(m)}}^{ij}\Pi_{{(m)}\alpha}^{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta_{{(m)}\lambda}\tilde{n}_{{(m)}}^{\alpha}-\sqrt{\tilde{g}_{{(m-1)}}}g_{{(m-1)}}^{ij}\Pi_{{(m-1)}\alpha}^{\lambda}\overline{\partial}{}_{i}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}\eta_{{(m-1)}\lambda}\tilde{n}_{{(m-1)}}^{\alpha}\right)

and
\Pi_{(m)}:=\hat{n}_{(m)}\otimes\hat{n}_{(m)}
.

We also define the energy functional of
([\eta]_{(m)},[v]_{(m)},[q]_{(m)})
to be

[\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}:=[\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}^{(1)}+[\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}^{(2)},

(8.40)

where

\displaystyle{}_{{(m)}}^{(1)}(T)

\displaystyle:=\left\|[\eta]_{(m)}\right\|_{3.5}^{2}+\left\|[v]_{(m)}\right\|_{3.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}[v]_{(m)}\right\|_{2.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}[v]_{(m)}\right\|_{1.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{3}[v]_{(m)}\right\|_{0}^{2}

(8.41)

\displaystyle+\left\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\right\|_{3.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\right\|_{2.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\right\|_{1.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\right\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle[\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}^{(2)}(T)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\partial_{t}^{3}[v]_{(m)}\cdot\tilde{n}_{(m)}\right|_{1}^{2}\,dt+\kappa\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{3}[v]_{{(m)}}\|_{1.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}\|_{1.5}^{2}\bigg).

8.2.1
The div-curl estimates

For
k=0,1,2

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}[v]_{(m)}\|_{3.5-k}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}[v]_{(m)}\|_{0}+\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{k}[v]_{(m)}\|_{2.5-k}+\|\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{k}[v]_{(m)}\|_{2.5-k}+|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}[v]_{(m)}\cdot N|_{2-k},

(8.42)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\|_{3.5-k}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\|_{0}+\|\text{div }\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\|_{2.5-k}

(8.43)

\displaystyle+\|\text{curl }\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\|_{2.5-k}+|\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}^{k}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\cdot N|_{2-k}.

Again, each part in the div-curl estimates should follow in the same way as in Section
3.2
so we omit the proof. For example, one can take
\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}}
in the equation of
[v]_{(m)}
to get the evolution equation of the curl:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}}[v]_{{(m)}})

\displaystyle-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\left(\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}}(\nabla_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}q_{(m)})-\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\partial_{t}\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}^{\mu\beta}\partial_{\mu}[v]_{{(m)}\gamma}-[(b_{0}\cdot\partial),\text{curl }_{\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}}]((b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}).

Then standard energy estimates give the control of curl part. One can control the divergence of
[v]
via
\text{div }_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}v_{(m)}
, and control the divergence of
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]
in the same way as in Section
3.2
. To control the interior terms in
[\mathcal{E}]_{(m)}^{(2)}
, we also need a similar div-curl decomposition for the
\kappa
-weighted terms and follow the method in Section
5
. Then we have

\displaystyle\kappa\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{3}[v]_{{(m)}}\|_{1.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}\|_{1.5}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\varepsilon[\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}(T)+P([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}(T),\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t),\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t))\,dt.

(8.44)

8.2.2
Elliptic estimates of pressure

The quantity
[q]_{(m)}
verifies the elliptic equation equipped with the Neumann boundary condition:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}}[q]_{(m)}=-\text{div }_{\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}}(\nabla_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}q_{(m)}-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}[\eta]_{(m)})+\partial_{t}\tilde{A}^{\mu\alpha}_{(m)}\partial_{\mu}[v]_{{(m)}\alpha}-\partial_{t}(\text{div }_{[\tilde{A}]_{(m-1)}}v_{(m)}),

\displaystyle\text{ in }\Omega,

(8.45)

\displaystyle\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}_{{(m)}}\tilde{A}^{\mu}_{{(m)}\alpha}\partial_{\mu}[q]_{m}=\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}_{{(m)}}(-\nabla_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}q_{(m)}-\partial_{t}[v]_{(m)}+(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}[\eta]_{{(m)}})_{\alpha},

\displaystyle\text{ on }\Gamma.

(8.46)

Compared to the estimates in Section
3.1
, we need to control the contribution of
\text{div}\,_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m)}}}(\nabla_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}q_{(m)})
on the RHS of (
8.45
), as well as the contribution of
\tilde{A}^{3\alpha}_{{(m)}}\nabla_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}q_{(m)}
on the RHS of (
8.45
), when estimating
\|[q]_{(m)}\|_{3.5}
, but this is straightforward.

\displaystyle\|\text{div }_{\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}}(\nabla_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}q_{(m)})\|_{1.5}\lesssim P(\|[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}\|_{2.5},\|q_{(m)}\|_{3.5},\|\tilde{A}_{(m)}\|_{2.5}),

(8.47)

In addition to this, for the boundary contribution, we have

\displaystyle|\tilde{A}_{{(m)}}N\cdot\nabla_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}q_{(m)}|_{2}\lesssim P(\|[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}\|_{2.5},\|q_{(m)}\|_{3.5},\|\tilde{A}_{(m)}\|_{2.5}).

(8.48)

The quantities
\|[\partial_{t}q]_{{(m)}}\|_{2.5}
,
\|[\partial_{t}^{2}q]_{{(m)}}\|_{1.5}
and
\|[\partial_{t}^{3}q]_{{(m)}}\|_{0}
are treated analogously by invoking the arguments in Section
3.1
.

8.2.3
Boundary estimates

The boundary estimates are parallel to those in Section
8.1.1
. Analogous to (
8.10
), we have

\kappa\overline{\Delta}{}([v]_{{(m)}}\cdot\tilde{n}_{{(m)}})=\kappa([v]_{{(m)}}\cdot\tilde{n}_{{(m)}})+h_{{(m)}}-[q]_{(m)}.

(8.49)

Then using the boundary elliptic estimates, we get

\displaystyle|[v]_{{(m)}}\cdot\tilde{n}_{{(m)}}|_{3}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}

\displaystyle|[v]_{{(m)}}\cdot\tilde{n}_{{(m)}}|_{1}+|h_{{(m)}}|_{1}+\|[q]_{(m)}\|_{1.5}

(8.50)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|[v]_{{(m)}}\cdot\tilde{n}_{{(m)}}|_{1}+\|[q]_{(m)}\|_{1.5}+|v_{(m)}|_{3}P(|\overline{\partial}{}[\tilde{\eta}]_{(m-1)},\overline{\partial}{}^{2}[\eta]_{(m-1)}|_{1},|\overline{\partial}{}\eta_{{(m-1)}}|_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t),\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t))\,dt.

As for the magnetic field, we use the fact that
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)=b_{0}^{j}\overline{\partial}{}_{j}
on
\Gamma
to get

(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}\cdot\tilde{n}_{{(m)}}=0+\int_{0}^{T}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[v]_{{(m)}}\cdot\tilde{n}_{(m)}+(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\cdot\partial_{t}\tilde{n}_{(m)}.

Similarly as in Section
8.1.1
, one can directly control the
H^{3}(\Gamma)
-norm of the second term. Then the first term can be controlled by using elliptic estimates in
(b_{0}\cdot\partial)
-differentiated elliptic equation (
8.49
). We omit the detailed proof because there is no essential difference from the arguments in Section
8.1.1
.

|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}\cdot\tilde{n}_{{(m)}}|_{3}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}P([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}(t),\mathcal{E}_{{(m)}}(t))\,dt.

(8.51)

Taking one time derivative, we can similarly control the boundary norm of
\partial_{t}[v]_{(m)}
and
\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}
. We skip the details.

\left|\partial_{t}[v]_{{(m)}}\cdot\tilde{n}_{{(m)}},\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}\right|_{2}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t),\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t))\,dt.

(8.52)

For the
H^{1}(\Gamma)
-norm of
\partial_{t}^{2}[v]_{(m)}
and
\partial_{t}^{2}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}
, one can use the
\kappa
-weighted interior terms in
[\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}^{(2)}
and Sobolev trace lemma to get the control

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}^{2}[v]_{{(m)}}^{3},\partial_{t}^{2}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}^{3}\right|_{1}\lesssim\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}[v]_{{(m)}},\partial_{t}^{2}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}\right\|_{1.5}

(8.53)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\left\|\partial_{t}^{3}[v]_{{(m)}}\cdot\tilde{n}_{{(m)}},\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}\right\|_{1.5}\,dt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\sqrt{\frac{T}{\kappa}}\left\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}[v]_{{(m)}},\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{y}^{1.5}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\sqrt{T}P([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}^{(2)}(T)).

Finally, we need to control the difference between
X\cdot N
and
X\cdot\tilde{n}_{(m)}
, which should be done in the same way as (
8.18
)-(
8.20
), so we do not repeat the calculations. For
k=0,1
, we have for
X=[v]_{(m)},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}

|\partial_{t}^{k}X^{3}-\partial_{t}^{k}(X\cdot\tilde{n}_{(m)})|_{3-k}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\int_{0}^{T}P([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}},\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t))\,dt.

(8.54)

Combining (
8.50
)-(
8.54
), we get the boundary estimates as

\sum_{k=0}^{2}\left|\partial_{t}^{k}([v]_{(m)}^{3},(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{{(m)}}^{3})\right|_{3-k}\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}\mathcal{P}_{0}+P(\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t),\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t))\,dt.

(8.55)

8.2.4
Estimates of full-time derivatives

Now it remains to control the
L^{2}
-norm of full time derivatives. By replacing
\partial_{t}^{4}
in Section
8.1.2
by
\partial_{t}^{3}
, we can do analogous computation to control
\|\partial_{t}^{3}[v]_{(m)}\|_{0}
and
\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\|_{0}
. The
\kappa
-weighted boundary terms in
[\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}^{(2)}
are produced in the analogues of (
8.31
). The only difference is that we should control the extra contribution (under time integral) of
\nabla_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}q_{(m)}
in the interior and the
\sigma
-coefficient part in the term
h_{(m)}
on the boundary. These quantities can all be directly controlled

\|\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla_{[\tilde{A}]_{{(m-1)}}}q_{(m)}\|_{0}\lesssim P\left(\|[v]_{(m-1)},\partial_{t}[v]_{(m-1)},\partial_{t}^{2}[v]_{(m-1)}\|_{2},\|\partial_{t}^{3}q_{(m)}\|_{1},\|\partial_{t}^{2}q_{(m)},\partial_{t}q_{(m)},q_{(m)}\|_{2}\right).

|\partial_{t}^{3}h_{(m),\sigma}|_{0}\lesssim P\left(|\partial_{t}^{2}v_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}|_{2},|\overline{\partial}{}\eta_{{(m)},{(m-1)}},\overline{\partial}{}v_{{(m)},{(m-1)}},\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}v_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}|_{L^{\infty}}\right).

Therefore, one can get

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{3}[v]_{(m)}\|_{0}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]_{(m)}\|_{0}^{2}+\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\partial_{t}^{3}[v]_{(m)}\cdot\tilde{n}_{(m)}\right|_{1}^{2}\,dt

(8.56)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}P([\mathcal{E}]_{(m)}(t),\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t))\,dt

8.3
Well-posedness of the nonlinear approximate problem

We conclude this section with the following proposition.

Proposition 8.3

(
Local well-posedness of the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation problem
)
.

For each fixed
\kappa>0
, there exists
T_{\kappa}^{\prime}>0
such that the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation problem (
3.2
) has a unique strong solution
(\eta(\kappa),v(\kappa),q(\kappa))
in
[0,T_{\kappa}^{\prime}]
that satisfies

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T_{\kappa}^{\prime}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(t)\leq\mathcal{C}

(8.57)

where
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(t)=\mathcal{E}^{(1)^{\prime}}(t)+\mathcal{E}^{(2)^{\prime}}(t)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(1)^{\prime}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\left\|\eta\right\|_{4.5}^{2}+\left\|v\right\|_{4.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}v\right\|_{3.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}v\right\|_{2.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\right\|_{1.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\right\|_{0}^{2}

(8.58)

\displaystyle+\left\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{4.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{3.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{2.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{1.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{(2)^{\prime}}(t)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\kappa}{\sigma}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\partial_{t}^{4}v\cdot\tilde{n}\right|_{1}^{2}\,dt+\kappa\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{4}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\|_{1.5}^{2}\bigg).

Proof.

Summarizing (
8.42
)-(
8.44
), (
8.55
)-(
8.56
), we can get the following inequality

\displaystyle{}_{{(m)}}(T)\lesssim_{\kappa^{-1}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0}+\varepsilon[\mathcal{E}](T)+TP([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}(T))

\displaystyle+P([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}(T),\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(T))\int_{0}^{T}P([\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t),\mathcal{E}_{{(m)},{(m-1)}}(t)).

By Gronwall-type inequality in Tao
[
53
]
and the conclusion of Proposition
8.1
, there exists some
T_{\kappa}^{\prime}>0
, such that
\forall t\in[0,T_{\kappa}^{\prime}]

[\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}(t)\leq\frac{1}{4}[\mathcal{E}]_{{(m-1)}}(t),

which implies
[\mathcal{E}]_{{(m)}}(t)\leq 4^{-m}\mathcal{P}_{0}
. Let
m\to\infty
, we know the sequence
\{(\eta_{(m)},v_{(m)},q_{(m)})\}
must strongly converge. The strong limit is denoted by
(\eta(\kappa),v(\kappa),q(\kappa))
which exactly solves the nonlinear
\kappa
-approximation problem (
3.2
). By taking
m\to\infty
in the energy of linearized equation (
7.2
), one can also get the energy estimates.
∎

9
Local well-posedness

9.1
Uniqueness and well-posedness

Combining the conclusions of Proposition
3.1
and Propostion
8.3
and letting
\kappa\to 0_{+}
, we actually prove that there exists some time
T^{\prime}>0
(only depends on the initial data), such that the original system (
1.13
) has solution
(\eta,v,q)
satisfying the energy estimates

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}E(t)\leq\mathcal{C},

where
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}(\|v_{0}\|_{4.5},\|b_{0}\|_{4.5},|v_{0}|_{5})
, and the energy functional
E
is defined to be

\displaystyle E(t):=

\displaystyle\left\|\eta\right\|_{4.5}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\left\|\left(\partial_{t}^{j}v,\partial_{t}^{j}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{4.5-j}^{2}+\left\|\left(\partial_{t}^{4}v,\partial_{t}^{4}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\right)\right\|_{0}^{2}

(9.1)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{3}\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left(\Pi\overline{\partial}{}^{3-j}\partial_{t}^{j}v\right)\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\big(\Pi\overline{\partial}{}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\big)\right|_{0}^{2},

To establish the local well-posedness, it remains to prove the uniqueness. Let
\{(\eta_{{(m)}},v_{(m)},q_{(m)})\}_{m=1,2}
be two solutions of (
1.13
) satisfying the energy estimates. Then we define

[\eta]:=\eta_{(1)}-\eta_{(2)},~[v]:=v_{(1)}-v_{(2)},~[q]:=q_{(1)}-q_{(2)},~[a]:=a_{(1)}-a_{(2)}.

Then
([\eta],[v],[q])
satisfies the following system

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}[\eta]=[v]~~~&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
\partial_{t}[v]-(b_{0}\cdot\partial)^{2}[\eta]+\nabla_{a_{(1)}}[q]=-\nabla_{[a]}q_{(2)}~~~&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
\text{div }_{a_{(1)}}[v]=-\text{div }_{[a]}v_{(2)},&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
\text{div }b_{0}=0~~~&\text{in}~[0,T]\times\Omega;\\
[v^{3}]=b_{0}^{3}=0~~~&\text{on}~\Gamma_{0};\\
[q]n_{(1)}=-\sigma g_{(1)}^{ij}\Pi_{(1)}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}_{ij}[\eta]-\sigma\sqrt{g_{(1)}}\Delta_{[g]}\eta_{(2)}~~~&\text{on}~\Gamma;\\
b_{0}^{3}=0~~~&\text{on}~\Gamma,\\
([\eta],[v])=(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0})~~~&\text{on}~\{t=0\}{\times}\overline{\Omega}.\end{cases}

(9.2)

Define

\displaystyle(t):=

\displaystyle\left\|[\eta]\right\|_{3.5}^{2}+\left\|[v]\right\|_{3.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}[v]\right\|_{2.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}[v]\right\|_{1.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{3}[v]\right\|_{0}^{2}

(9.3)

\displaystyle+\left\|(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]\right\|_{3.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]\right\|_{2.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{2}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]\right\|_{1.5}^{2}+\left\|\partial_{t}^{3}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)[\eta]\right\|_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left(\Pi_{(1)}\partial_{t}^{2}[v]\right)\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left(\Pi_{(1)}\overline{\partial}{}\partial_{t}[v]\right)\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\left(\Pi_{(1)}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}v\right)\right|_{0}^{2}+\left|\overline{\partial}{}\big(\Pi_{(1)}\overline{\partial}{}^{2}(b_{0}\cdot\partial)\eta\big)\right|_{0}^{2}.

Then we can mimic the proof in Section
3
to get the energy estimates of
[E]

[E](T)\lesssim P([E](T),E(T))\int_{0}^{T}P([E](t),E(t))\,dt,

which together with Gronwall-type inequality yields

\exists T\in[0,T^{\prime}],~~[E](t)=0~~\forall t\in[0,T]

which establishes the local well-posedness of (
1.13
) in
[0,T]
.

9.2
Regularity of initial data and free surface

Finally, we need to prove that the norms of time derivatives can be controlled by
\|v_{0}\|_{4.5},\|b_{0}\|_{4.5}
and
|v_{0}|_{5}
. This part is exactly the same as in Section
6.1
or
[
38
, Section 7.1]
. Finally, the boundary condition of (
1.13
) gives us an elliptic equation of
\eta
on
\Gamma

-\sigma\sqrt{g}\Delta_{g}\eta^{\alpha}=a^{3\alpha}q.

By using elliptic estimates in Dong-Kim
[
16
]
(see also
[
13
, Proposition 3.4]
), one has

|\eta|_{5}\lesssim|a^{3\alpha}q|_{3}\lesssim|(\overline{\partial}{}\eta\times\overline{\partial}{}\eta)q|_{3}\lesssim P(\|\eta\|_{4.5})\|q\|_{3.5}.

Similarly, taking a time derivative gives us the elliptic equation of
v^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\overline{\partial}{}_{ij}^{2}v^{\alpha}=

\displaystyle\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Gamma_{ij}^{k}\overline{\partial}{}_{k}v^{\alpha}-\partial_{t}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij})\overline{\partial}{}_{ij}^{2}\eta^{\alpha}-\partial_{t}(\sqrt{g}g^{ij}\Gamma_{ij}^{k})\overline{\partial}{}_{k}\eta^{\alpha}

\displaystyle-\sigma^{-1}(\partial_{t}a^{3\alpha}q+a^{3\alpha}\partial_{t}q),

and thus by the similar argument in
[
38
, Section 5.1]
we get

|v(t)|_{5}\lesssim P(E(t))~~~~\text{in }[0,T].

This concludes the proof of Theorem
1.2
.
